endpoint](research/current2011-ordinary69-84-ipra/README.md), [independently
reviewed](reviews/current2011-ordinary69-84-ipra-independent/README.md), checks
both mapped-refutation and mapped Gödel–Gentzen consistency formulations.
The graph/file comparison and degree remain external checks.
A proposed one-gate reduction of the 71/80 sphere leaves an extra `W = -1`
case. A [transport-only obstruction](research/sphere70-negative-transport-obstruction/README.md),
[independently reviewed](reviews/sphere70-negative-transport-independent/README.md),
shows that positive transport inputs alone do not rule this out. It neither
constructs a full graph zero nor disproves fixed-port existential equivalence.
A [separate bounded screen](research/sphere-factor-partition-screen/README.md)
checks all 52 partitions in a specified unshared five-factor square-sum family;
an [independent audit](reviews/sphere-factor-partition-screen-independent/README.md)
reproduces its operation counts and exact specialized degrees. None of that
family's screened profiles improves the established operation–degree tradeoffs;
the screen neither proves zero equivalences nor a global circuit lower bound.
A [current-port 67-operation/degree-94 equation](research/current2011-ordinary67-94-candidate/README.md)
improves the 67/106 tradeoff. Its [independent audit](reviews/current2011-ordinary67-94-candidate-independent/README.md)
checks the graph, exact fixed-byte degree and conditional forward algebra.
A [current-base Lean endpoint](research/current2011-ordinary94-fresh-endpoint/README.md),
[independently audited](reviews/current2011-ordinary94-fresh-endpoint-independent/README.md),
establishes the existential no-zero bridge and standard-iPRA equivalence.
The reverse direction passes through machine soundness and may change witnesses;
it is not a same-valuation equivalence. Fixed-byte degree and graph-to-source
correspondence are external checks, and provider transitive ancestry remains
inherited. A [translated-consistency client](research/current2011-ordinary94-fresh-translated/README.md),
[independently reviewed](reviews/current2011-ordinary94-fresh-translated-independent/README.md),
also checks the standard-iPRA equivalence with Foundation's mapped Gödel–Gentzen
consistency sentence on that base.

For the 13-variable 70/140 and 71/128 forms, a [current-port graph comparison](research/thirteen-current2011-forms-frontier/README.md)
and [independent audit](reviews/thirteen-current2011-forms-frontier-independent/README.md)
match the complete graphs with the historically checked Lean source and verify
exact degrees for the recorded coefficient bytes. The recorded historical
endpoint provider is currently incomplete, so its audit could not be replayed;
the new graph comparison is not itself a fresh Lean endpoint or a file theorem.
A [fresh-base build attempt](research/thirteen-current2011-fresh-model/REVIEW-MAP.md)
compiled seven predecessors before the adapted carrier hit its memory cap.
Its import-only, split-core, prelude and selected helper checks are recorded
with [independent](reviews/thirteen-current2011-split-helpers-independent/README.md)
[audits](reviews/thirteen-current2011-build-diagnostics-independent/README.md).
An unchanged-statement [4-GB retry](research/thirteen-current2011-complete-4g-retry/README.md),
[independently audited](reviews/thirteen-current2011-completion-4g-independent/README.md),
subsequently checked the 13/69 completion and native/standard bridge; its
resource monitoring is sampled and that run did not print a separate axiom
report. The adapted [13/70 and 13/71 Forms module](research/thirteen-current2011-forms-4g/README.md)
now checks on that provider, and its [independently reviewed](reviews/thirteen-current2011-final-checks-4g-independent/README.md)
[axiom-report client](research/thirteen-current2011-final-checks-4g/README.md)
checks the two standard-iPRA mapped-refutation equivalences. Their graph and
exact degree assertions remain external, as do file-content comparisons. A
[translated-consistency client](research/thirteen-current2011-translated-4g/README.md),
[independently reviewed](reviews/thirteen-current2011-translated-4g-independent/README.md),
also checks both mapped Gödel–Gentzen consistency formulations.
The [13-variable 69-operation form](research/thirteen-current2011-69-174/README.md)
has exact degree 174 for the recorded coefficient bytes by a separate
[independent graph audit](reviews/thirteen-current2011-69-174-independent/README.md).
Its [fresh-base Lean client](research/thirteen-current2011-69-endpoints-4g/README.md),
[independently reviewed](reviews/thirteen-current2011-69-endpoints-4g-independent/README.md),
checks both standard-iPRA mapped-refutation and mapped Gödel–Gentzen endpoints.
An [external syntax comparison](research/current2011-ordinary67-94-syntax-binding/README.md),
[independently checked](reviews/current2011-ordinary67-94-syntax-binding-independent/README.md),
matches the entire candidate graph to the existing `Carrier94Syntax` source.
A [source-closure survey](research/current2011-ordinary94-source-closure-survey/README.md)
identified six additional modules for the current-base existential no-zero
bridge; the [six-module build](research/current2011-ordinary94-six-module-build/README.md)
and its [independent review](reviews/current2011-ordinary94-six-module-build-independent/README.md)
record the compiled sources, including the extraction source variant.
A [checked partial Pell obstruction](research/ordinary67-relaxed-same-f-obstruction/README.md),
[independently reviewed](reviews/ordinary67-relaxed-same-f-obstruction-independent/README.md),
shows the relaxed divisibility hypotheses alone do not justify restoring the
old multiplier while keeping `f` and `c` fixed. It does not rule out a
different witness. The existing `ArithmeticPellAuxiliarySeed.auxiliary_seed`
constructs a replacement strong and auxiliary seed under its stated index and
phase hypotheses. The checked endpoint instead uses the existing index recovery
and machine-soundness development for its existential reverse direction.
A separately reviewed [66-operation rational evaluator](research/normalized106-rational66/README.md)
computes the same degree-106 polynomial using one nonintegral fixed leaf;
its operation count does not include construction or storage of fixed leaves,
and the integer-leaf graph remains at 67 operations. Its [Lean full-output
binding](research/normalized106-rational66-lean/README.md) checks equality
to the 67/106 syntax over commutative rings under the two fixed-leaf
identities, on the historical provider.
An [actual-port specialization](research/normalized106-rational66-actual/README.md)
proves that the rational evaluator and integer syntax have the same positive
zeros at the same valuation. A [combined client](research/normalized106-rational66-pair/README.md)
checks this external equivalence alongside the existing integer-syntax iPRA
derivability theorem, and a [same-provider replay](research/normalized106-rational66-pair-replay/README.md)
matches its object and log. These are separate conjuncts: they do not express
the rational evaluator as an iPRA formula or supply a 66-operation integer
equation. Their historical package and transitive ancestry remains inherited;
the materialized numerical coefficient bytes are not formally identified with
the Lean ports by this result.

A different [66-operation integer-leaf candidate](research/current2011-unscaled66/README.md)
has 14 positive variables and exact degree 138 at the recorded current2011
ports. Its complete polynomial times `Delta` equals the maintained 65/150
polynomial. An [independent exact audit](reviews/current2011-unscaled66-independent/README.md)
checks this identity, the gate count and degree, including a nonzero leading
coefficient on a univariate substitution; `Delta` is positive on the intended
domain. This trades one extra operation for degree 138. A [Lean client](research/current2011-unscaled66-model/README.md)
checks the literal syntax, the factor identity, the all-model same-zero bridge
and standard-iPRA derivability on a historical provider. Its [independent
review](reviews/current2011-unscaled66-model-independent/README.md) checks all
66 graph rows and the recorded direct inputs; historical package-transitive
and dynamic ancestry remain inherited. A subsequent [current14-base client](research/current2011-unscaled66-fresh-base/README.md)
checks the same literal graph, model bridge and iPRA theorem using the new
source-built 106 extension; its [independent audit](reviews/current2011-unscaled66-fresh-base-independent/README.md)
checks all graph rows and the recorded direct inputs. The degree audit is external, and
recorded coefficient bytes have not been formally identified with the Lean
ports.
For the first port β, a [Lean decoding theorem](research/current2011-beta-byte-binding/README.md)
identifies an explicit big-endian byte recipe with the current2011 port; an
[independent audit](reviews/current2011-beta-byte-binding-independent/README.md)
also compares that recipe with both recorded compressed payloads externally.
For MC, a separate [six-run digit recipe theorem](research/current2011-mc-byte-binding/README.md)
identifies its canonical base-256 decoding with the third Lean port; an
[independent audit](reviews/current2011-mc-byte-binding-independent/README.md)
reconstructs the value and compares both retained payloads externally. Lean
does not read those files. For K, an explicit [physical-order selector recipe](research/current2011-kernel-byte-recipe/README.md)
is proved in Lean to decode to port 1, and an [independent audit](reviews/current2011-kernel-byte-recipe-independent/README.md)
reconstructs and compares the recorded bytes externally. Thus all four ports
have symbolic Lean byte-decoding theorems plus separate external payload
comparisons; none is a Lean theorem about gzip or filesystem contents. The
four decoders have also been joined in two [checked pairs](research/current2011-four-port-byte-pairs/README.md).
A [fully parametric assembly lemma](research/current2011-four-port-byte-generic/README.md)
and a [concrete four-port tuple theorem](research/current2011-four-port-byte-tuple/README.md)
now check in Lean; an [independent audit](reviews/current2011-four-port-tuple-success-independent/README.md)
verifies the recorded source, predecessor objects and successful exit. Three
earlier tuple attempts failed at the memory cap and remain recorded.
For shifted MF, [partial diagnostics](research/current2011-mf-byte-binding/README.md)
check the unshifted digit and beta identities in Lean and compare the once-
shifted recipe with both payloads externally. The [independent review](reviews/current2011-mf-partial-binding-independent/README.md)
confirms the remaining shifted-port composition did not pass within the
recorded memory limit. A later [split decoder](research/current2011-mf-decoder-explicit-term/README.md),
[independently audited](reviews/current2011-mf-decoder-independent/README.md),
checks the once-shifted symbolic byte-list decoding to port 3 in Lean. The
comparison of that recipe with the two compressed files is still external.
On the historical provider, a [translated-consistency client](research/current2011-unscaled66-translated/README.md)
also checks the standard-iPRA equivalence with Foundation's mapped
Gödel–Gentzen consistency sentence, with an [independent audit](reviews/current2011-unscaled66-translated-independent/README.md).
The same formulation has since been checked on the [current14 base](research/current2011-unscaled66-fresh-translated/README.md)
and independently audited [there](reviews/current2011-unscaled66-fresh-translated-independent/README.md).
A [joint current-base client](research/current2011-unscaled66-vs106-fresh/README.md)
checks that the 66/138 and 67/106 shifted expressions have the same zeros
at each valuation in every IΣ₁ model, as well as a separate ring identity
showing their values coincide when the strong norm is one. Its [review](reviews/current2011-unscaled66-vs106-fresh-independent/README.md)
covers the recorded direct inputs and successful run.

For the distinct 12-variable, 95-operation free-J graph, a bounded author
build now checks an [unconditional all-model equivalence](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/compact44-symbolic-model-unconditional-iff-prototype/README.md):
in each model of IΣ₁, its fixed symbolic positive-zero predicate is
equivalent to the encoded machine's internal halting predicate. All four
symbolic port projections are discharged, including the single beta shift in
the high port. A [source review](reviews/compact44-symbolic-model-unconditional-iff-source-review/README.md)
checks the final join, with disclosed overlap in authorship and without a
fresh replay or complete ancestry audit.

**In the Lean tree (3 November 2026).** That equivalence's 472-module
closure was [promoted into `Lean/DiophantineConsistency/Compact44/`](reviews/compact44-source-promotion/README.md):

- the copies change only import lines, and each is hash-pinned to its research
  source;
- the closure builds against current sources;
- [`Compact44/IPRAOrdinary12`](Lean/DiophantineConsistency/Compact44/IPRAOrdinary12.lean)
  joins it to the positive-model adapter.

The result is native, standard and equality-only iPRA proofs that the shifted
12-variable graph has no zero iff ZFC is consistent. Each proof reports only
`propext`, `Classical.choice` and `Quot.sound`. This is an author build, not a
paired replay. The binding of the Lean literal to the retained payload bytes
and the PDF stays external, as below.

**Smaller 12-variable graphs in the tree (3 November 2026).** The 94-, 92-,
91-, 90- and 89-operation graphs described below now have native, standard and
equality-only iPRA endpoints in
[`Compact44/IPRAOrdinary12Chain`](Lean/DiophantineConsistency/Compact44/IPRAOrdinary12Chain.lean).
Their exact degrees are 84, 164, 172, 172 and 172.

- Their 87-module research closure was
  [promoted the same way](reviews/ordinary12-chain-source-promotion/README.md).
- All 15 endpoints report only `propext`, `Classical.choice` and `Quot.sound`.

This supersedes the "not yet established" and "not yet compiled" remarks
below for these five graphs. It is still an author build, and the JSON/PDF
binding remains external.

A [12-variable, 94-operation variant](research/ordinary12-sum-of-squares-candidate/README.md)
replaces the final unit-factor product with a sum of three residual squares.
Lean checks same-valuation zero equivalence to the 95-operation expression in
every IΣ₁ model, and a separate [model client](research/ordinary12-sum-squares-model-bridge/README.md)
checks both positive-coordinate and shifted byte-recipe zero iff internal
halt. An external exact-degree check gives 84, versus the parent's stated 70;
94 counts the unshifted positive-coordinate evaluator, not the separate
`x+1` substitutions. The JSON/source and file/PDF correspondence remain
external, and an iPRA derivation for this variant is not yet established.
An [independent source and arithmetic review](reviews/ordinary12-sum-squares-independent-review/README.md)
checks the same-valuation model reduction, the 94-row graph, and the degree
calculation within the stated provider limits; it is not a fresh Lean replay.
[Standalone decimal displays](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/compact44-free-j-display-v2/README.md)
and their [independent review](reviews/ordinary12-decimal-displays-v2-independent-review/README.md)
cover the 94- and 95-operation variants. These are external finite checks,
not Lean proofs of PDF or file semantics. A proposed [94-operation iPRA
client](research/ordinary12-sum-squares-ipra-endpoint-draft/README.md) has
not yet been compiled on a coherent provider.
A proposed [92-operation quotient graph](research/ordinary12-qcube92-candidate/README.md)
replaces the old positive witness `Y` by `q³s` and divides the first
residual by `q⁶`. An [independent finite audit](reviews/ordinary12-qcube92-independent/README.md)
checks its graph, polynomial scaling identities and fixed-coefficient exact
degree 164. A bounded author Lean check now proves the [three residual
identities](research/ordinary12-qcube92-candidate/ALGEBRA.md) over arbitrary
commutative rings, with the inherited-provider limits recorded there. An
independently reviewed [source and artifact check](reviews/ordinary12-qcube92-algebra-independent/README.md)
compares all 92 Lean graph rows and the recorded identities. A separate
[model theorem](research/ordinary12-qcube92-candidate/MODEL.md) proves that
the new zero is equivalent to an old zero at the transformed valuation in
every IΣ₁ model, for arbitrary natural ports. A further bounded Lean check
proves [positive-zero existence equivalence](research/ordinary12-qcube92-candidate/EXISTENCE.md)
between the 92- and 95-operation graphs in every IΣ₁ model, for natural ports
with positive β, using the existing `q³ ∣ Y` theorem. An [independent review](reviews/ordinary12-qcube92-existence-independent/README.md)
checks the witness maps, hypotheses and retained provider evidence. A
separately checked [fixed-port halt join](research/ordinary12-qcube92-candidate/HALT.md)
now establishes the symbolic, shifted and closed Lean byte-recipe model
equivalences; its [independent audit](reviews/ordinary12-qcube92-halt-independent/README.md)
checks the selected provider and recorded result. Full inherited ancestry
remains open, as do the 92-operation graph's iPRA endpoint and formal PDF
binding. The [proposed iPRA client](research/ordinary12-qcube92-ipra-endpoint-draft/README.md)
has not yet been compiled. A [standalone decimal display](research/ordinary12-qcube92-decimal-display/README.md)
has external checks of all 92 rows, fixed-coefficient recipes and selector
blocks; these do not constitute a Lean theorem about the PDF.

A proposed [91-operation graph](research/ordinary12-xq91-candidate/README.md)
replaces another positive witness `X` by `q w` and factors two expressions.
The [independent graph and degree audit](reviews/ordinary12-xq91-independent/README.md)
checks all live rows and gives an external exact degree of 172. A bounded
Lean check establishes the [three residual identities](research/ordinary12-xq91-candidate/ALGEBRA.md)
over arbitrary commutative rings. A [fixed-port model halt join](research/ordinary12-xq91-candidate/HALT.md)
now uses the checked symbolic alignment theorem for the converse witness
map and establishes positive, shifted and closed Lean byte-recipe forms in
every IΣ₁ model. It is an author build with the recorded inherited-ancestry
limits, not yet an internal iPRA derivation. An [independent audit](reviews/ordinary12-xq91-halt-independent/README.md)
checks its theorem, fixed-port assumptions and selected union. The [iPRA client](research/ordinary12-xq91-ipra-endpoint-draft/README.md)
remains an unchecked draft. A [standalone decimal display](research/ordinary12-xq91-decimal-display/README.md)
and its [independent review](reviews/ordinary12-xq91-decimal-independent/README.md)
externally check the 91 rows and fixed-coefficient recipes; these are not
Lean theorems about PDF or filesystem semantics.

A [90-operation direct-main graph](research/ordinary12-direct-main-probe/GRAPH.md)
replaces `gamma+w` with a single positive input. Lean now checks its
[positive-zero equivalence](research/ordinary12-direct-main-probe/EQUIVALENCE.md)
to the 91-operation graph under the stated port bounds and its fixed-port
[all-model halt bridge](research/ordinary12-direct-main-probe/HALT.md),
including shifted and closed Lean byte-recipe forms. The graph and exact
degree 172 are externally checked. The [iPRA client](research/ordinary12-direct90-ipra-endpoint-draft/README.md)
passed a guarded [author join](research/ordinary12-direct90-ipra-join/ATTEMPT03.md),
with an [independent receipt audit](reviews/ordinary12-direct90-ipra-join-independent/README.md).
It states no-premise derivability in the standard and equality-only theories,
for both the mapped native refutation negation and the selected translated
consistency sentence. The audit is not a fresh Lean replay; package-transitive,
dynamic and complete historical source-to-object ancestry remain inherited.
The [decimal display](research/ordinary12-direct90-decimal-display/README.md)
has external graph, payload and PDF checks; no Lean PDF semantics is claimed.

An [89-operation absorbed-first-root draft](research/ordinary12-absorb89-probe/README.md)
has an externally checked live gate count and Lean-checked forward algebraic
comparison. Its graph-specific model lemmas obtain both signed first-root
branches, transport divisibility, negative-branch reflection at the first
root, and a positive-branch growth bound conditional on a Pell index at least
3. A [six-stage index chain](research/ordinary12-absorb89-probe/INDEX_CHAIN.md)
now derives an index of at least 13 from an actual graph zero with the stated
port bounds. A [positive-branch theorem](research/ordinary12-absorb89-probe/POSITIVE_BRANCH.md)
uses that index to prove the absorbed witness exceeds `s`; a separate
[model reflection](reviews/ordinary12-absorb89-reflection-independent/README.md)
preserves the full graph output in the negative branch. Their
[existence-equivalence join](research/ordinary12-absorb89-probe/EQUIVALENCE.md)
now proves that the 89- and 90-row graphs have positive zeros in the same
IΣ₁ models under those port bounds. An [independent receipt audit](reviews/ordinary12-absorb89-equivalence-independent/README.md)
checks the theorem scope and selected provider without replaying Lean. A subsequent [fixed-port halt bridge](research/ordinary12-absorb89-probe/HALT.md)
checks the positive, shifted and byte-port forms. A guarded [iPRA join](research/ordinary12-absorb89-ipra-join/README.md)
now proves the standard and equality-only equivalences for the shifted Lean
byte-port graph, and an [independent receipt audit](reviews/ordinary12-absorb89-ipra-join-independent/README.md)
has passed without an independent Lean replay. Package-transitive, dynamic,
and complete historical source-to-object ancestry remain inherited. A
[base-10 decimal display](research/ordinary12-absorb89-decimal-display/README.md)
externally checks the 89 rows, coefficient payloads and exact degree 172;
there is no Lean parser or theorem about the TeX or PDF.

An [88-operation absorbed-root draft](research/ordinary12-absorb88-probe/README.md)
has an externally checked live graph and Lean-checked forward ring identities.
The [first-factor extraction](research/ordinary12-absorb88-probe/FIRST.md)
is now checked in models, including a positive Pell root when beta is positive.
The [signed-root alternatives](research/ordinary12-absorb88-probe/SIGNS.md)
and [positive-branch witness bound](research/ordinary12-absorb88-probe/GAP.md)
are checked conditional on the unresolved transport bound.
Lean also checks [restricted existence equivalence](research/ordinary12-absorb88-probe/RESTRICTED.md):
a positive 89 zero exists exactly when an 88 zero exists that satisfies the
explicit transport bound.
Its reverse argument is open: exact first-factor examples show the needed
transport positivity cannot be derived from that factor alone, including one
under the abstract port bounds. Neither example is a full-graph counterexample.
An external [symmetry argument](reviews/ordinary12-absorb88-transport-obstruction/README.md)
further shows that, if a fixed-port 89 zero exists, there is a full 88 zero
with negative transport. This rules out a universal positivity proof under
that condition, but does not settle existential equivalence.
A [two-norm bound](research/ordinary12-absorb88-main-root-draft/README.md)
now applies to actual positive 88 zeros with positive beta, giving
`eta+zeta >= 8XY^2+4` without a transport-sign assumption; it has a
separate [source review](reviews/absorb88-two-norm-gap-independent/README.md).
A [main-root orientation inequality](research/ordinary12-absorb88-negative-main-orientation-draft/README.md)
is checked under explicit ordered-ring bounds. On the negative-transport
branch, a separate [actual-graph signed-rank bound](research/ordinary12-absorb88-negative-rank-bound-draft/README.md)
now supplies the needed lower-bound form. Their [checked graph composition](research/ordinary12-absorb88-negative-main-graph-draft/README.md)
proves positivity of the expanded signed main expression on that branch.
A [later Lean binding](research/ordinary12-absorb88-main-evaluation-draft/README.md)
identifies it with the evaluated main register and transfers the positivity.
A further [oriented Pell balance](research/ordinary12-absorb88-main-balance-draft/README.md)
selects the positive scaled root on the negative branch, with an independent
[source review](reviews/absorb88-main-balance-independent/README.md). These
results inherit temporary providers. A further [checked signed-rank congruence](research/ordinary12-absorb88-signed-rank-congruence-draft/README.md)
derives `q^4 ∣ (k−R−1)` on the negative branch, in the model difference
ring. A further [first-index binding](research/ordinary12-absorb88-signed-early-index-draft/README.md)
obtains `n≥2` and `q^4 ∣ (2n−R−1)` without identifying `n` with the rank.
An [exact partial-factor example](research/ordinary12-absorb88-normalization-obstruction/README.md)
shows that the first factor, these scalar bounds and the signed congruence
alone do not force `G`-preserving positive transport; its other graph
residuals do not vanish. A [guarded normalization](research/ordinary12-absorb88-guarded-normalization-draft/README.md)
constructs an 89 zero from an 88 zero when explicit decomposition and
positivity witnesses exist. Deriving those witnesses from every relevant
negative-branch zero, the rank window and an unconditional 89 witness remain
open. A [checked necessary-condition theorem](research/ordinary12-absorb88-normalization-guard-obstruction-draft/README.md)
shows that this particular guard requires `F≥q` and `alpha≥1+b²`. An
[external conditional construction](research/ordinary12-absorb88-one-step-conditional-obstruction/README.md)
would produce a full 88 zero violating the guard from a positive 89 zero
with an additional divisibility property; existence of such a zero has not
been established. This does not rule out another transformation or
existence equivalence. No 88-operation halt or iPRA endpoint is claimed.

A more aggressive [86-operation signed experiment](research/ordinary12-absorb86-probe/README.md)
has externally checked liveness and Lean ring identities for the transformed
residuals; an [independent audit](reviews/ordinary12-absorb86-signed-graph-independent/README.md)
checks those selected artifacts without replaying Lean. Its proposed input map can be negative even on positive inputs;
positive-zero equivalence, halt and iPRA remain unproved.

For the separate ten-positive 84- and 85-operation proposals, a
[Pell-doubling prototype](research/ten-positive-ipra-doubling-prototype/README.md)
proves coprimality of abstract doubled radicands in every IΣ₁ model, using
explicit Bézout certificates. A subsequent [product-root theorem](research/ten-positive-ipra-product-root-prototype/README.md)
combines that result with scaled-square extraction to recover positive roots
from the exact abstract cross-multiplied relation. It is not yet bound to
either graph; full root recovery, the machine bridge and an iPRA endpoint
remain open.

The [79-row coordinate probe](research/ten-positive-79-mask-coordinate-probe/README.md)
replaces the third positive unknown by the mask C and has an external all-ring
substitution identity to the 80-row graph. A [Lean ring client](research/ten-positive-79-ring-probe/README.md)
now checks that identity for arbitrary commutative rings and ports, with an
external row comparison. An [independent ring audit](reviews/ten-positive-79-ring-independent/README.md)
checks the row mapping, direct provider and theorem scope without replaying
Lean. Its reverse positive map requires
`F+C<q`, which is not established; it is not a proved smaller consistency
equation or iPRA endpoint. A [guarded model client](research/ten-positive-79-guarded-model-probe/README.md)
now proves 79-zero implies 80-zero when that inequality is supplied explicitly;
the unguarded implication remains open. A [guarded iff](research/ten-positive-79-guarded-model-probe/FORWARD.md)
also constructs the other direction and proves 80 positive-zero existence
equivalent to 79 positive-zero existence *with* that guard, for positive beta.
An [independent guarded audit](reviews/ten-positive-79-guarded-and-bfs-cyclic-independent/README.md)
checks that premise and the separate cyclic-field receipts without replaying Lean.
A [follow-up iff/parity audit](reviews/ten-positive-79-forward-bfs-parities-independent/README.md)
checks both guarded directions and the BFS field-parity receipts; it also does
not replay Lean or eliminate the guard.
A separate [74-row trace probe](research/trace74-mask-coordinate-probe/README.md)
applies the same substitution to the upstream 75-row BFS-kernel chart. Its
[Lean ring identity](research/trace75-mask-coordinate-ring-probe/README.md)
is checked for arbitrary ports, with JSON comparison external. The parent
trace75 now has a [fixed-symbolic-port iPRA proof-existence assembly](research/trace75-bfs-ipra-final-assembly-plan/RUNNER.md)
on its compatible captured provider, with the external payload and inherited
ancestry qualifications described in the [trace75 status](research/ten-positive-ipra/PLAN.md).
An [external degree certificate](research/trace74-degree-check/README.md)
establishes exact degree 402 for its pinned coefficients; an [independent audit](reviews/trace74-ring-coordinate-independent/README.md)
checks that certificate and the row/identity receipts without replaying Lean.
A [guarded trace model iff](research/trace75-mask-coordinate-ring-probe/GUARDED.md)
equates positive 75 and 74 zeros only with **both** respective coordinate
guards explicit (`F+alpha<q` on the old side, `F+C<q` on the new side).
Neither guard is derived within that guarded iff. A later [model theorem](research/trace75-root-probe/TO74.md)
derives the old-chart guard from a positive trace75 zero and obtains its
equivalence with a positive trace74 zero **with the new-chart guard**. The
guard for an arbitrary positive fixed-port trace74 zero remains open, as do
its unguarded iPRA endpoint and formal external-file binding. The newer
[trace74 status](research/trace74-mask-coordinate-probe/README.md) records
the checked guard-free signed-rank, projection and index-window results.
An [independent trace-guard audit](reviews/trace74-guarded-iff-independent/README.md)
checks the original guarded directions and selected provider evidence without replaying Lean.
A [proposed fixed-port coupled-Pell witness](research/trace74-symbolic-coupled-pell-strategy/README.md)
would violate the guard if its explicit external gcd and binding assumptions
hold; the complete zero is not kernel checked. Recent model-level components
connect a [CRT-updated natural tuple to the first Pell inputs](research/trace74-positive-carrier/Trace74CRTPellFactorJoin.md)
and verify a [binary Pell residue evaluator](research/trace74-pell-bitlist-checker/README.md).
The concrete coprimality certificates, CRT modulus and rank binding, full
positive witness, and unguarded equivalence remain open. These Lean checks
inherit prebuilt-provider ancestry.

The later ten-positive 80-operation, degree-750 descendant has an
[80-row Lean syntax and model-root theorem](research/ten-positive-80-model-binding-prototype/README.md).
For arbitrary natural ports with positive beta, any positive zero yields the
two positive doubled-factor roots; the graph rows are compared to the stored
JSON externally. A [further model theorem](research/ten-positive-80-pell-prototype/README.md)
derives the first Pell index, the doubled strong-Pell identity and a required
divisibility from such a zero. An [independent receipt and source audit](reviews/ten-positive-80-root-pell-independent/README.md)
checks these statements and their selected inputs without replaying Lean.
At this initial stage adjacent-Pell descent, the machine bridge, iPRA
derivability and formal JSON/PDF binding were open; later progress and the
distinction between original and BFS kernels are recorded below. The 81-, 83- and 84-operation
descendants share the externally checked finalizer shape but are distinct
graphs. A separate [adjacent-Pell denominator lemma](research/ten-positive-adjacent-descent-prototype/README.md)
proves that a reduced denominator's square divides the corresponding
coefficient under the stated cross-multiplied equation. It does not yet prove
the descent or denominator integrality required by the 80-row argument. A
[separate step theorem](research/ten-positive-adjacent-preserve-prototype/README.md)
establishes reducedness of the transformed numerator, integrality of the
transformed first coordinate and a ring norm identity. A subsequent
[complete descent step](research/ten-positive-adjacent-step-prototype/README.md)
proves a positive strictly smaller coordinate at the same reduced denominator.
An [internal least-number theorem](research/ten-positive-adjacent-complete-prototype/README.md)
now proves that the denominator is one under the reduced norm hypotheses.
An [integral-quotient theorem](research/ten-positive-adjacent-integer-prototype/README.md)
constructs the reduced representation from the abstract scaled norm, without
assuming divisibility. The [80-row adjacent join](research/ten-positive-80-adjacent-prototype/README.md)
now derives the required positive integral quotient and adjacent Pell norm
from an actual positive graph zero. An [independent adjacent-chain audit](reviews/ten-positive-80-adjacent-independent/README.md)
checks the seven source/receipt stages and their provider limits without a Lean
replay. A [strong/auxiliary index theorem](research/ten-positive-80-folding-prototype/README.md)
now supplies the strong and odd adjacent indices without assuming the main
norm. A [graph-specific signed congruence](research/ten-positive-80-residue-binding-prototype/README.md)
is checked and [joined to the same magnitude witness](research/ten-positive-80-folding-joined-prototype/README.md)
as the indices. A [folding-input corollary](research/ten-positive-80-folding-consequences-prototype/README.md)
then gives `c≤I` and the signed congruence between `c` and the auxiliary
ordinate. An [independent audit](reviews/ten-positive-80-indices-residue-independent/README.md)
checks the common witness and noncircular source dependencies without replaying
Lean. A separate [noncircular all-index recovery theorem](research/pell-all-index-recovery-prototype/README.md)
now proves ordinate recovery in every IΣ₁ model under explicit strict size
bounds, using internal antiperiodicity and odd-index folding. A subsequent
[graph-specific fold](research/ten-positive-80-folding-complete-prototype/README.md)
derives those bounds and the odd main ordinate from a positive 80-row zero,
without assuming its main Pell norm. An [independent fold audit](reviews/pell-all-index-recovery-independent/README.md)
checks the same witnesses, noncircular source dependencies and receipts
without a Lean replay. A [main-norm corollary](research/ten-positive-80-main-norm-prototype/README.md)
derives that norm as a conclusion. Rank decoding, machine calibration and the
iPRA endpoint remain open. A [signed rank-index theorem](research/ten-positive-80-rank-index-prototype/README.md)
now derives the actual graph rank's signed congruence to its odd auxiliary
index without assuming a rank sign. A [rank-reduction join](research/ten-positive-80-rank-main-prototype/README.md)
relates the signed graph rank to the recovered odd main index modulo `c`;
the [conditional equality theorem](research/ten-positive-80-rank-equality-prototype/README.md)
derives rank equality if `2|R|<c`. A [corrected scaled-growth lemma](research/ten-positive-80-scaled-growth-prototype/MINUS.md)
handles the graph's main parameter but still assumes index alignment and the
stated size bounds. Its [graph application](research/ten-positive-80-scaled-growth-prototype/GRAPH.md)
obtains the two Pell equations from an actual zero but keeps alignment and
`X≥16` as explicit premises; a [size lemma](research/ten-positive-80-scaled-growth-prototype/SIZE.md)
derives the latter from `beta≥15`. A separate [first-index theorem](research/ten-positive-80-scaled-growth-prototype/INDEX_ONE.md)
derives index at least two from a positive zero at positive beta, without
assuming rank, transport or alignment. This yields the actual graph bound
[`X<c`](research/ten-positive-80-scaled-growth-prototype/C_ABOVE_X.md) and a
positive correction gap. A [model carrier lemma](research/ten-positive-carrier-transport-prototype/README.md)
checks a conditional negative-transport contradiction. The exact graph
[carrier identity](research/ten-positive-80-carrier-identity-prototype/README.md),
[signed integral carrier](research/ten-positive-80-signed-carrier-prototype/README.md),
and [conditional graph transport theorem](research/ten-positive-80-transport-conditional-prototype/README.md)
are now checked. An [offset bound](research/ten-positive-80-offset-bound-prototype/README.md)
supplies one of its two size estimates from positive inputs; a separate
[carrier estimate](research/ten-positive-80-carrier-estimate-prototype/README.md)
supplies the other for its returned graph witnesses. The [same-witness transport join](research/ten-positive-80-transport-model-prototype/README.md)
now proves nonnegative transport and the strict mask gap for every positive
graph zero with positive beta, without assuming signs. An [independent receipt audit](reviews/ten-positive-80-transport-independent/README.md)
checks the same-witness composition and selected producers without replaying
Lean. Fixed-port rank bounds,
machine soundness and the iPRA endpoint remain open. A further
[rank-orientation theorem](research/ten-positive-80-rank-orientation-prototype/README.md)
derives positivity of the signed graph rank from this transport result, and
a [natural-rank binding](research/ten-positive-80-natural-rank-prototype/README.md)
identifies its exact positive natural value. A [rank-window packet](research/ten-positive-80-rank-window-prototype/README.md)
proves the symbolic window and `2R<c` under the stated port bounds and mask
gap. The [exact rank join](research/ten-positive-80-rank-exact-prototype/README.md)
uses those bounds to identify the natural rank with the positive odd main
Pell index. An [independent audit](reviews/ten-positive-80-rank-window-independent/README.md)
checks the universal representation argument and stated port assumptions
without replaying Lean. Fixed-byte recipe calibration, machine equivalence
and iPRA remain open. The graph's signed
carrier additionally supplies [`μ² ∣ h*e`](research/ten-positive-80-carrier-cancel-h-prototype/README.md)
after an explicit cancellation of `H`; canceling `h` still requires the
separate radix-parity/unit argument. A [graph parity packet](research/ten-positive-80-rank-parity-prototype/README.md)
constructs that unit certificate under the port bounds and an explicit
odd-beta witness. A [symbolic fixed-beta parity join](research/ten-positive-80-rank-parity-prototype/FIXED.md)
discharges beta positivity and oddness for `CompactFixedBeta.betaNat` while
retaining the low/high port inequalities. A separate [signed cancellation join](research/ten-positive-80-offset-divisibility-prototype/README.md)
now derives `μ² ∣ e` under explicit positive-beta, low/high and odd-beta
assumptions. A [first-index residue theorem](research/ten-positive-80-first-index-residue-prototype/README.md)
uses this to obtain `2n ≡ rank+1 (mod μ²)` for an actual first Pell index.
A [noncircular alignment theorem](research/ten-positive-80-index-alignment-prototype/README.md)
now excludes other residue representatives and proves `2n=rank+1` under the
explicit symbolic port and odd-beta assumptions. Fixed-recipe composition
remains separate. A subsequent [exact offset theorem](research/ten-positive-80-offset-exact-prototype/README.md)
uses the full first recurrence to derive a nonnegative offset and the stronger
signed divisibility `(μ²Y²) ∣ e`, without assuming coprimality of `Y` and `h`.
A [positive-offset theorem](research/ten-positive-80-positive-offset-prototype/README.md)
further obtains a positive natural quotient of `e` by `μ²` under those
symbolic assumptions.

The [80-row height theorem](research/ten-positive-80-scaled-growth-prototype/POWER.md)
combines the actual alignment and scaled-growth lemmas to derive `2^rank<Y`
from a positive graph zero, with beta size/oddness and symbolic low/high bounds
explicit. An [independent audit](reviews/ten-positive-80-index-alignment-independent/README.md)
checks the same alignment witness, quotient bound and selected producers
without replaying Lean. A separate [projection-divisibility theorem](research/ten-positive-80-projection-divisibility-prototype/README.md)
derives `H ∣ h*Kpos` in the difference ring; a [natural projection theorem](research/ten-positive-80-natural-projection-prototype/README.md)
now transfers that divisibility back to the model's natural elements. A
[Pell projection join](research/ten-positive-80-projection-pell-prototype/README.md)
packages the natural divisor and main coordinate identities at the checked
rank. An [abstract whole-product theorem](research/ten-positive-80-projection-product-prototype/README.md)
derives the corresponding ring divisibility without canceling the carrier
factor. Its [graph binding](research/ten-positive-80-projection-product-prototype/GRAPH.md)
now proves the same signed divisibility for actual positive zeros under the
symbolic port bounds. An [independent projection audit](reviews/ten-positive-80-projection-independent/README.md)
checks the retained factor and selected receipts without replaying Lean.
A [conditional final step](research/ten-positive-80-projection-product-prototype/HEIGHT.md)
proves `X=2^rank` if the entire product is strictly smaller than `H`. A
[variable-base height theorem](research/ten-positive-80-variable-height-prototype/README.md)
now derives `X^(r+1)<H` for an actual zero with `rank=2r+1` under the symbolic
port and oddness assumptions. The [graph power join](research/ten-positive-80-projection-product-prototype/GRAPH_POWER.md)
now bounds the complete product and proves `X=2^rank` under explicit beta-size,
oddness and low/high port bounds. An [independent power audit](reviews/ten-positive-80-height-power-independent/README.md)
checks its common witness, retained carrier factor and selected providers
without replaying Lean. A [symbolic specialization](research/ten-positive-80-fixed-symbolic-power-prototype/README.md)
derives the power and aligned indices from three explicit port equalities. A
separate [radix alignment](research/ten-positive-80-projection-product-prototype/RADIX.md)
obtains the power-of-two block form under an explicit beta identity. Full
fixed tuple/file binding and machine/iPRA equivalence remain separate.
The [repunit join](research/ten-positive-80-repunit-prototype/README.md)
recovers `J` using the same block-count witness. A preliminary
[scaled Pell sandwich](research/pell-scaled-sandwich-prototype/README.md)
checks the lower ratio inequality and shifted coefficient gap; a
[graph join](research/ten-positive-80-sandwich-lower-prototype/README.md)
applies that lower bound to actual positive graph zeros. A separate
[upper sandwich](research/pell-scaled-sandwich-upper-prototype/README.md)
and [rounding theorem](research/ten-positive-scaled-rounding-prototype/README.md)
are checked under explicit inequalities. A [population join](research/ten-positive-80-population-sandwich-prototype/README.md)
derives the half-binomial identity and population bound if both strict
sandwiches hold for the same graph index. A subsequent [graph sandwich join](research/ten-positive-80-sandwich-both-prototype/README.md)
now derives both strict inequalities for one actual graph index. The
[population composition](research/ten-positive-80-population-model-prototype/README.md)
uses that witness to derive the half-binomial identity and population bound
from an actual zero, without assuming either sandwich or the identity. Its
symbolic beta and radix hypotheses remain explicit. An [independent audit](reviews/ten-positive-80-both-sandwich-independent/README.md)
checks the same-index joins and selected receipts without replaying Lean;
machine decoding and iPRA remain separate. A [saturation theorem](research/ten-positive-80-saturation-prototype/README.md)
derives the two no-carry conclusions for the same repunit length, conditional
on explicit repeated-mask arithmetic facts. A [symbolic-mask specialization](research/ten-positive-80-fixed-saturation-prototype/README.md)
now discharges those facts under three explicit port equalities; external
tuple/file binding and machine decoding remain separate. An [independent audit](reviews/ten-positive-80-saturation-independent/README.md)
checks the same-length saturation and selected mask facts without replaying
Lean. A [fixed-digit join](research/ten-positive-80-fixed-digits-prototype/README.md)
adds the exact natural transport, origin digit and bounded low-mask digits
for the same graph zero and radix length. A [slotwise join](research/ten-positive-80-fixed-slots-prototype/README.md)
extends the low-mask no-carry conclusion to every nonzero slot. An [independent audit](reviews/ten-positive-80-fixed-digits-independent/README.md)
checks these witnesses and the locally compiled increment proof without a Lean
replay. A [fixed congruence join](research/ten-positive-80-fixed-congruence-prototype/README.md)
reconciles the digit and radix lengths and derives the transport congruence.
The [rank-phase theorem](research/ten-positive-80-rank-phase-prototype/README.md)
now derives rank `≡3 (mod 4)` under the symbolic port equalities. A separate
[indexed-carry join](research/ten-positive-80-indexed-carry-prototype/README.md)
reaches the generic compact carry interface with all four port equalities.
**Kernel mismatch:** the retained 80-row JSON fixes its `K0` to the directly
indexed BFS payload (raw SHA-256 `0a86821a704294199c2e1a50379d242c96840a3366e9d965689ba3d74605203f`),
whereas the existing external transcription of `paramKernel rho` matches the
original-order K payload (raw SHA-256 `c6d523bd32af8dd0345db843cdadf6932bfcad186912a6d112314b60657c554e`).
Those payloads differ. Therefore the decoder results requiring
`p 1 = paramKernel rho` cannot currently be instantiated to the retained
80-row JSON's K0. Either adapt the decoder to the directly indexed kernel or
construct and separately verify an original-kernel graph specialization.
The three-port fresh-Pell results below leave `p 1` arbitrary; this mismatch
does not by itself affect those statements. The external transcription is not
a Lean theorem identifying either file's bytes with the symbolic term.
A separately labeled [original-kernel 80-row candidate](research/ten-positive-80-original-kernel-variant/README.md)
retains the same row schedule with the complete original coefficient tuple;
it is a candidate only, with its file binding, machine theorem and iPRA endpoint
still to be established. Its degree 750 has a separate external modular
diagonal-witness check and an [independent candidate review](reviews/ten-positive-80-original-kernel-variant-independent/README.md),
not a Lean degree theorem. The BFS instance also
remains a target. A separate [direct-BFS symbolic kernel](research/direct-bfs-lean-kernel-prototype/README.md)
now checks its 943-row data and model casts in Lean, with an external byte
comparison to the BFS K. It does not yet provide row/color conjugacy, a
complete graph decoder, controller calibration, or a Lean file-binding
theorem. A [local selector decoder](research/direct-bfs-lean-decoder-prototype/README.md)
also checks the generic no-carry decoding equivalence for the BFS rows; it
does not yet connect those local facts to a graph band. A
[finite mass calculation](research/direct-bfs-lean-mass-prototype/README.md)
and [model lift](research/direct-bfs-lean-model-mass-prototype/README.md)
check the direct coefficient-family bounds needed for carry estimates. The
[BFS band/carry theorem](research/direct-bfs-lean-band-carry-prototype/README.md)
and [graph carry join](research/direct-bfs-80-carry-prototype/README.md)
now derive the indexed carry family from the actual positive 80 graph under
the direct-BFS symbolic kernel port and the other three symbolic port
equalities. These author checks do not prove the external port/file binding,
selected-column decoding or machine/color conjugacy. An
[80-graph support join](research/direct-bfs-80-support-prototype/README.md)
reconciles the radix length with the kernel-independent low-mask support and
Boolean facts. A [coefficient decoder](research/direct-bfs-lean-coefficient-decoder-prototype/README.md)
connects the direct selector coefficients to the local no-carry decoder,
and a [raw-column theorem](research/direct-bfs-lean-raw-column-prototype/README.md)
expresses the full carry column with the direct tile payloads. The selected
graph-column and machine conclusions are still separate. A receipt audit
found that the coefficient-decoder runner did not individually pin its direct
`ArithmeticWindowCarry.olean` import; a post-hoc hash does not establish its
compile-time bytes. A corrected author runner and fresh raw-column, raw-parity
and graph-parity checks have since passed with bit-identical objects. An
[independent v2 audit](reviews/direct-bfs-carry-decoder-v2-independent/README.md)
checks the selected direct inputs and provider edges without replaying Lean.
A [selected-origin lemma](research/direct-bfs-lean-selected-origin-prototype/README.md)
proves the direct table's index-zero tile agrees with the corresponding old
origin tile and reuses the generic origin occupancy conclusion after that
tile is selected; it does not identify the full tables or kernels. A
[direct-BFS occupancy join](research/direct-bfs-80-occupied-anchor-prototype/README.md)
now derives all seven origin slots from the actual graph under the direct-BFS
symbolic port equalities, and an [alignment join](research/direct-bfs-80-aligned-shift-prototype/README.md)
proves the temporal shift divisible by 67. A [whole-cell join](research/direct-bfs-80-cell-shift-prototype/README.md)
derives the cell offset modulo the cycle, and a [uniform reset join](research/direct-bfs-80-uniform-reset-prototype/README.md)
derives the selected temporal and carry resets at the same length. These are
author checks; complete window and machine decoding remain separate. An
independent receipt check found that the cell-shift runner did not
individually pin its direct `SymbolicKBandDraft.olean` import. Its post-hoc
hash is not evidence of the compile-time bytes. Corrected cell-shift and
reset author checks now pin and preflight all direct imports, and produce
bit-identical objects; an [independent v3 audit](reviews/direct-bfs-cell-reset-v3-independent/README.md)
checks all selected direct imports and producer edges without replaying Lean. The
[independent receipt review](reviews/direct-bfs-cell-and-80-source-data-independent/README.md)
records this gap and checks the source-data witness scope without replaying
Lean. An
[arbitrary-phase local decoder](research/direct-bfs-lean-uniform-decoder-prototype/README.md)
and [actual-graph join](research/direct-bfs-80-uniform-decoder-prototype/README.md)
now establish the direct-table selector/copy decoding at every designated
band phase under the direct symbolic ports. A [field-bits join](research/direct-bfs-80-field-bits-prototype/README.md)
derives the vertical and horizontal parity families at the same length. An
[independent review](reviews/direct-bfs-uniform-and-positive-seed-independent/README.md)
checks the two uniform-decoder receipts and the generic positive seed without
replaying Lean; a [field-bit audit](reviews/direct-bfs-field-bits-independent/README.md)
checks its generic and graph receipts and same-length scope. Full Wang-window
and machine/color conjugacy remain open. An
[edge-field theorem](research/direct-bfs-lean-tile-fields-prototype/README.md)
identifies all four binary payload slices with the direct table's typed edge
labels in the stated models. A [direct occupied-orbit lemma](research/direct-bfs-lean-block-orbit-prototype/README.md)
propagates occupancy under explicit decoder, marker and edge hypotheses; its
binding to an actual graph zero and full tile window remain separate. An
[independent orbit audit](reviews/direct-bfs-tile-fields-orbit-independent/README.md)
checks the selected receipts and distinguishes the generic arbitrary-step
orbit premise from the separately proved unit-successor step, without a Lean
replay. An
[marker-parity lemma](research/direct-bfs-lean-marker-parity-prototype/README.md)
handles the direct selector family under explicit support and alignment
assumptions. A separate axiom-free [color-bounds check](research/direct-bfs-lean-color-bounds-prototype/README.md)
shows both tables stay inside the finite color domains, without proving the
maps are permutations or all rows match. An
[actual-graph marker join](research/direct-bfs-80-field-markers-prototype/README.md)
derives the direct vertical and horizontal field equalities including their
marker bits under the four explicit direct-BFS symbolic port equalities. An
[independent marker/color audit](reviews/direct-bfs-markers-colors-independent/README.md)
checks selected direct inputs and the shared graph witness without replaying
Lean. A [spatial-successor join](research/direct-bfs-80-spatial-successor-prototype/README.md)
derives an occupied base and cyclic next-block propagation from an actual
graph zero. An [all-block join](research/direct-bfs-80-all-blocks-prototype/README.md)
propagates it to a selected direct tile at every internal block. These are
author checks; an [independent all-blocks audit](reviews/direct-bfs-spatial-all-blocks-independent/README.md)
checks the common length and unit-successor propagation without replaying Lean.
The [represented-word client](research/direct-bfs-80-all-block-word-prototype/README.md)
constructs one internal base-1024 word of selected direct tile indices at
every block, using a [direct orbit word](research/direct-bfs-lean-orbit-row-prototype/README.md).
An [independent word audit](reviews/direct-bfs-orbit-row-word-independent/README.md)
checks the same-index representation and selected receipts without Lean replay.
A [field-slice theorem](research/direct-bfs-lean-field-slices-prototype/README.md)
and [graph tile-matching join](research/direct-bfs-80-tile-match-prototype/README.md)
then derive cyclic horizontal and aligned temporal vertical edge matching
for that direct-table word under explicit symbolic ports. This is not yet
the model-level color/machine interpretation, converse, iPRA or file binding.
An [independent edge audit](reviews/direct-bfs-field-slices-tile-match-independent/README.md)
checks the same N,W,s, selected indices and eight direct imports without a
Lean replay; it does not assert `s>0` or boundary/machine behavior. An
[internal local-word join](research/direct-bfs-80-local-word-prototype/README.md)
now composes represented direct rows, origin/exclusion facts and local edge
equalities at one length. Translating this direct-table word to the original
machine table, the converse, iPRA and formal file binding remain open. A
[model-valued inverse index map](research/direct-bfs-model-index-map-prototype/README.md)
preserves represented bounded sequences, and [finite edge reflection](research/direct-bfs-lean-edge-reflection-prototype/README.md)
maps matching direct edges back to the original typed colors. These author
results are joined by a [model word-transfer theorem](research/direct-bfs-model-word-transfer-prototype/README.md)
that maps a direct local word to an original-table local word, preserving
origin, exclusion and both edge equalities. An [actual-graph word join](research/direct-bfs-80-original-word-prototype/README.md)
applies that transfer to a positive 80 zero at the direct-BFS symbolic ports.
A [BFS graph-to-halt join](research/direct-bfs-80-model-halt-prototype/README.md)
composes the transferred word with selected machine soundness providers to
derive an internal halt time under four direct-BFS symbolic port equalities.
This author check has not yet established a coherent full provider closure,
the converse, or external byte binding. A [selected-header scan](research/direct-bfs-80-model-halt-serialized-audit/README.md)
captures 739 project modules, reconciles 529 with the prior 90-provider
record and checks the 59 conjugacy chunks; full historical, package-transitive
and dynamic ancestry remains outside its scope. An [independent scan audit](reviews/direct-bfs-model-halt-serialized-independent/README.md)
rechecks the selected paths, companions and reported overlaps without replaying Lean.
The reverse path now has [finite edge transport](research/direct-bfs-lean-edge-forward-prototype/README.md),
a [model forward index map](research/direct-bfs-model-forward-map-prototype/README.md),
and a [model forward-word theorem](research/direct-bfs-model-forward-word-prototype/README.md)
that transfers the original local tiling predicate to the direct table.
An [internal halt-to-direct-word join](research/direct-bfs-halt-word-prototype/README.md)
now produces that direct local word from an internal halt and the existing
power-based geometry. A [model payload lookup](research/direct-bfs-model-payload-prototype/README.md)
adds definable direct-table fields and identifies them with the symbolic tile
payload on standard row indices. The direct-family packing and graph-witness
construction are still separate. A [cell-digit theorem](research/direct-bfs-cell-digit-prototype/README.md)
adds jointly definable Boolean selector, dummy and payload-copy digits; it
does not itself construct packing. A [represented-cell theorem](research/direct-bfs-packed-cell-prototype/README.md)
now packs and recovers those digits in a bounded definable family; global
packing and its carry/mask conditions remain open. A [constructed-word theorem](research/direct-bfs-constructed-word-prototype/README.md)
now packs arbitrary definable row/dummy families and recovers selectors and
copies with Boolean and origin bounds. The global packing input's low/high
mask and carry conditions still need proof. A [halt-to-packed-word join](research/direct-bfs-halt-packed-word-prototype/README.md)
and [definable dummy-pulse extension](research/direct-bfs-halt-packed-pulse-prototype/README.md)
construct such words from internal halt while preserving normalized dummy
digits. A [same-row pulse family](research/direct-bfs-halt-packed-family-prototype/README.md)
keeps one halt-derived row and geometry while quantifying over the pulses;
an [independent ingredient audit](reviews/direct-bfs-halt-packing-ingredients-independent/README.md)
checks this quantifier order and the conditional transport premises without
replaying Lean. A [local low-mask lemma](research/direct-bfs-cell-low-pulse-prototype/README.md)
handles normalized pulses for non-forbidden rows. Global origin, high-mask
and rank calibration remain separate. A [word low-mask join](research/direct-bfs-word-low-fields-prototype/README.md)
proves the positive origin and low-mask fields for a direct local word when
the dummy at the origin is even. A [conditional packing adapter](research/direct-bfs-conditional-packing-prototype/README.md)
combines those fields with explicitly assumed transport/high-mask conditions;
those conditions and calibrated graph witnesses remain open. A [direct-cell size bound](research/direct-bfs-cell-size-prototype/README.md)
proves a smaller upper bound under non-forbidden-row and Boolean-dummy
assumptions. A [sharp-cell refinement](research/direct-bfs-cell-sharp-size-prototype/README.md)
provides the bound needed for high-mask padding. A [direct kernel bound](research/direct-bfs-kernel-bound-prototype/README.md)
now proves the symbolic kernel is below the stated power without expansion;
a [kernel divisibility theorem](research/direct-bfs-kernel-divisibility-prototype/README.md)
also proves radix divisibility and the resulting model mod-four fact;
a [lower-part theorem](research/direct-bfs-kernel-lower-bound-prototype/README.md)
separates the distinguished high term and bounds the remainder, as a
prerequisite for high-mask arguments; a [lower-mass refinement](research/direct-bfs-kernel-lower-mass-prototype/README.md)
and its [exact-definition alias](research/direct-bfs-kernel-lower-mass-alias-prototype/README.md)
supply a stronger bound needed for [high-padding size](research/direct-bfs-high-padding-size-prototype/README.md).
A [phase-zero theorem](research/direct-bfs-phase-zero-high-prototype/README.md)
proves high-mask no-carry for that phase; a [padding-tail theorem](research/direct-bfs-high-padding-prototype/README.md)
handles the terminal zero digits. An [active-phase packing reduction](research/direct-bfs-active-phase-packing-prototype/README.md)
reduces the remaining high-mask proof to six explicitly listed phase classes,
which remain assumptions. This is not yet completeness;
the [constructed carry](research/direct-bfs-constructed-carry-prototype/README.md)
and [temporal carry](research/direct-bfs-temporal-constructed-carry-prototype/README.md)
clients provide the direct cyclic product-carry inputs from explicit word
bounds, without assuming carry vanishes. A [Boolean stencil theorem](research/direct-bfs-stencil-zero-carry-prototype/README.md)
proves zero cyclic carry when its explicit Boolean-family and carry-input
premises hold; an [aligned-word join](research/direct-bfs-aligned-zero-carry-prototype/README.md)
now establishes zero carry for the constructed direct-BFS word and temporal
term. A [physical-column theorem](research/direct-bfs-physical-column-prototype/README.md)
identifies each physical block digit with its direct raw column using this
zero-carry result. A [cyclic field theorem](research/direct-bfs-word-field-cyclic-prototype/README.md)
derives matching payload bits and source digits from a direct local word; its
runner automatically enforces only the final two of six captured axiom reports.
A [physical field-parity theorem](research/direct-bfs-word-field-parity-prototype/README.md)
derives evenness at nine vertical and eight horizontal field phases without a
high-mask assumption. A [marker-parity client](research/direct-bfs-word-marker-parity-prototype/README.md)
adds the adjacent marker positions, covering both full field ranges; the
[field no-carry join](research/direct-bfs-word-field-high-prototype/README.md)
now proves the required high-mask condition on both ranges. The four anchor
and target phases were separate; an [anchor client](research/direct-bfs-word-anchor-high-prototype/README.md)
now proves no-carry at 2195 and 3296. Only target phases 4393 and 5495 remain
open. An [occupied-aggregate theorem](research/direct-bfs-word-occupied-prototype/README.md)
computes the direct selector/copy sum as `1+2^51` for each local-word row;
the [target-phase join](research/direct-bfs-marked-target-prototype/README.md)
uses it to prove no-carry at both 4393 and 5495. All listed phase lemmas have
now been checked separately. A [constructed-word packing theorem](research/direct-bfs-word-packing-prototype/README.md)
assembles them into `PackingInput` with no remaining high-mask premise, under
explicit direct local word, definability, origin-dummy, geometry and scalar
mask bounds. An [independent packing audit](reviews/direct-bfs-word-packing-independent/README.md)
checks these same-witness assumptions and selected phase receipts without
replaying Lean. A [canonical halt-packing client](research/direct-bfs-halt-canonical-packing-prototype/README.md)
now derives the direct packing family from internal halt under three explicit
scalar mask bounds, keeping one row and geometry for all admissible dummies.
Calibrated rank and graph witnesses remain separate. A [controller residue check](research/direct-bfs-controller-residue-audit/README.md)
externally confirms that the BFS kernel's residue differs from the original
kernel's, while its candidate Γ residue is a unit. Neither this arithmetic nor
the BFS file-to-symbolic identity is yet a Lean calibration theorem. A
[symbolic residue theorem](research/direct-bfs-symbolic-k-residue-prototype/README.md)
now checks the BFS Lean kernel's residue itself. A [finite Γ-order theorem](research/direct-bfs-gamma-order-prototype/README.md)
checks the modular order identity; the model-level Γ unit and full rank
calibration remain separate. An [independent packing/residue audit](reviews/direct-bfs-canonical-packing-residue-independent/README.md)
checks the same-row quantifiers, scalar premises and external residue arithmetic
without replaying Lean;
a [predecessor lemma](research/direct-bfs-origin-predecessor-prototype/README.md)
shows the relevant vertical predecessor is not the marker and its packed cell
is divisible by four. An [origin-block reduction](research/direct-bfs-origin-block-residue-prototype/README.md)
uses this to derive the adjusted high-origin field from an explicit all-index
ordinary high-mask condition. A [high-only packing join](research/direct-bfs-high-only-packing-prototype/README.md)
discharges the checked size and positivity fields but retains that all-index
high-mask premise and scalar geometry; the premise remains open;
a [cell-product client](research/direct-bfs-cell-product-prototype/README.md)
uses it and the cell bound to prove the conditional `hi_lt` field for a
non-forbidden normalized cell. A [four-field adapter](research/direct-bfs-four-fields-packing-prototype/README.md)
discharges that product field while retaining explicit cell/block sum, origin
and both high-mask assumptions. A [block-size theorem](research/direct-bfs-kernel-block-size-prototype/README.md)
now proves the cell-sum field for a suitably bounded direct cell family and
identifies the high-product split. A [block-positivity theorem](research/direct-bfs-word-block-positive-prototype/README.md)
proves positive blocks for a direct local word and normalized dummy; the
high-mask calibration remains separate. An [independent bounds audit](reviews/direct-bfs-packing-bounds-residue-independent/README.md)
checks the selected receipts and explicit size/stride/high-mask premises
without replaying Lean. An
[array-shape check](research/direct-bfs-lean-conjugacy-shape-prototype/README.md)
proves the candidate row/color map sizes and selected distinguished entries,
but not the full inverse maps or row correspondences. A
[bounded conjugacy sweep](research/direct-bfs-lean-conjugacy-coverage-prototype/README.md)
has independently reviewed coverage and successful endpoint pilots; its
full 59-stage Lean author run has exited successfully with all ranges accepted.
An [independent sweep audit](reviews/direct-bfs-conjugacy-full-sweep-independent/README.md)
checks all 59 receipts, objects and required axiom reports without replaying Lean.
The first [aggregate attempt](research/direct-bfs-lean-conjugacy-aggregate-prototype/README.md)
failed, but a separate [retry](research/direct-bfs-lean-conjugacy-aggregate-retry-prototype/README.md)
has checked all seven range-aggregate Boolean declarations with outer exit 0.
An [independent accepted-aggregate audit](reviews/direct-bfs-conjugacy-aggregate-accepted-independent/README.md)
checks its selected 60 direct providers and axiom reports without replaying
Lean. A [pointwise finite client](research/direct-bfs-lean-conjugacy-lift-prototype/README.md)
then extracts row matching and bijections on the typed finite row/color
indices, with original-to-direct orientation explicit. It remains an author
check; an [independent finite-lift audit](reviews/direct-bfs-conjugacy-lift-independent/README.md)
checks its mapping direction, aggregate provider and selected receipts without
replaying Lean. The internal word and tiling lift remain separate.
The [sweep audit](reviews/direct-bfs-conjugacy-sweep-v2-independent/README.md)
documents its single-run discipline, pinned inputs and explicit limits. An
[independent graph-carry audit](reviews/direct-bfs-80-carry-independent/README.md)
checks the selected producer receipts and same-length interface without
replaying Lean. An
[independent audit](reviews/direct-bfs-lean-kernel-decoder-independent/README.md)
checks selected kernel/decoder/mass receipts and reconstructs the BFS K bytes
externally; it does not replay Lean.
An [independent mismatch review](reviews/ten-positive-80-alignment-fresh-projection-independent/README.md)
checks the two payloads and the selected alignment and fresh-projection
receipts; it does not replay Lean.
An [independent audit](reviews/ten-positive-80-phase-indexed-carry-independent/README.md)
checks these phase and carry receipts and their stated witness and port boundaries;
it does not replay Lean or establish the complete decoder.
A [support/carry join](research/ten-positive-80-support-carry-prototype/README.md)
adds the compact support and Boolean digit facts at the same length. A
[parity join](research/ten-positive-80-simple-parity-prototype/README.md)
derives both compact anchor-column carry parities, and a separate
[clean-carry theorem](research/ten-positive-80-clean-carry-prototype/README.md)
finds a band position with zero incoming carry and an exact output digit.
These are guarded author checks, with no independent Lean replay; full window
decoding and machine soundness remain open. An [occupied-anchor join](research/ten-positive-80-occupied-anchor-prototype/README.md)
derives all seven origin slots, and an [alignment join](research/ten-positive-80-aligned-shift-prototype/README.md)
derives divisibility of the temporal shift by 67 for the same length. The
subsequent [cell-shift join](research/ten-positive-80-cell-shift-prototype/README.md)
places the shift on a whole cell at that length. These are author checks;
the full window and machine soundness still remain open.
A [half-binomial join](research/ten-positive-80-half-binomial-prototype/README.md)
obtains the identity at the actual graph rank under the beta bounds, without
assuming fixed symbolic ports or a radix exponent. The generic
[shared-floor lemma](research/ten-positive-pell-sandwich-floor-prototype/README.md)
retains a scaled sandwich's small remainder. A subsequent
[fresh-interval theorem](research/ten-positive-80-fresh-interval-prototype/README.md)
uses it to prove the unscaled Pell pair lies strictly between consecutive
multiples of the actual graph's Y, at the same rank. This does not identify
the fresh ordinate with the graph's scaled ordinate.
The [positive interval join](research/ten-positive-80-fresh-first-main-prototype/README.md)
and [fresh norm join](research/ten-positive-80-fresh-pell-norms-prototype/README.md)
construct positive first/main Pell witnesses and their norm equations at the
same rank, X and Y. A [fresh projection](research/ten-positive-80-fresh-projection-prototype/README.md)
adds the positive main-root projection, and a [fresh offset](research/ten-positive-80-fresh-main-offset-prototype/README.md)
derives its natural divisibility remainder without assuming it is positive.
A [factor identity](research/ten-positive-fresh-main-algebra-prototype/README.md)
and [90-row main-factor client](research/ten-positive-90-main-factor-prototype/README.md)
show a conditional residual zero for the selected source graph under explicit
projection and norm hypotheses. A later [80-to-90 main-factor join](research/ten-positive-80-to-90-main-prototype/README.md)
discharges those hypotheses with one positive 12-coordinate valuation from
an actual 80-row zero, while leaving p₁ arbitrary. Only its main factor is
proved zero; compatible witnesses for the other factors and a whole-graph
source zero remain open.
A [flexible main-factor join](research/ten-positive-80-to-90-main-flexible-prototype/README.md)
keeps its fresh main witnesses fixed while allowing any positive choices for
the four coordinates unused by the main factor. An audit found that the
earlier fresh-offset runner did not individually pin its direct
`ArithmeticPellCongruences.olean` import; its compile-time bytes are not
authenticated by the later path/hash observation. A corrected runner and
both dependent rechecks have now passed with the missing import individually
pinned and bit-identical output objects. An [independent v2 audit](reviews/ten-positive-80-to-90-main-v2-independent/README.md)
checks these selected receipts and direct inputs without replaying Lean. The
[independent main-factor review](reviews/ten-positive-80-to-90-main-independent/README.md)
records the historical limitation and checks the flexible witness scope
without replaying Lean.
A [positive transport join](research/ten-positive-80-positive-transport-prototype/README.md)
derives `q < v₅` and a strictly positive natural transport quotient from the
actual positive 80-graph zero, still with p₁ arbitrary. This is a prerequisite
for constructing further source factors, not a source-zero theorem.
A [fresh first-coordinate join](research/ten-positive-80-fresh-first-coordinate-prototype/README.md)
adds a positive first-coordinate witness and first Pell norm to the same
unscaled main witnesses and positive transport quotient. Its abstract
[inequality lemma](research/ten-positive-first-coordinate-prototype/README.md)
and the graph join do not yet establish the actual first-factor zero. An
independent receipt check found that the abstract lemma's runner did not
individually pin its direct `ArithmeticPellFirstNorm.olean` import; its
post-hoc object hash cannot establish the earlier compile-time bytes. A
corrected author run and dependent recheck have now passed with bit-identical
objects; an [independent v2 audit](reviews/ten-positive-80-fresh-first-coordinate-v2-independent/README.md)
checks the selected inputs and rebuilt provider edges without replaying Lean. See the
[independent first-coordinate review](reviews/ten-positive-80-fresh-first-coordinate-independent/README.md).
A subsequent [first/main join](research/ten-positive-80-to-90-first-main-prototype/README.md)
zeros both selected 90-graph factors using one positive valuation from the
same actual 80 zero, with three other positive coordinates still arbitrary
and p₁ unrestricted. A [source-data join](research/ten-positive-80-to-90-source-data-prototype/README.md)
exposes the exact unscaled Pell data for the same witnesses to support the
remaining construction. An [independent first/main audit](reviews/ten-positive-80-to-90-first-main-independent/README.md)
checks selected receipts, same-witness scope and the BFS occupancy client
without replaying Lean. A later [ordinary-factor identity](research/ten-positive-90-ordinary-factor-prototype/README.md)
and [three-factor join](research/ten-positive-80-to-90-full-zero-prototype/README.md)
construct one positive 90 valuation that zeros all three selected factors.
The [output wrapper](research/ten-positive-80-to-90-output-prototype/README.md)
then proves, in every stated IΣ₁ model, that an actual positive 80 Lean graph
zero implies a positive selected 90 Lean graph output zero at the **same**
arbitrary p₁, under the three symbolic β/low/high port assumptions. These
are guarded author checks; an [independent receipt audit](reviews/ten-positive-80-to-90-full-zero-independent/README.md)
checks their direct inputs and same-output witness without replaying Lean. They do not prove
the reverse direction, equate the distinct external K payloads, bind either
file to Lean, or establish a halt or iPRA theorem.
A separate [forward halt join](research/ten-positive-80-symbolic-halt-forward-prototype/README.md)
composes the 80-to-90 result with the selected 90-row halt theorem. It assumes
the three β/low/high equalities **and** an explicit equality of the entire
integer port tuple to the original-kernel 90-row symbolic tuple. Thus it is a
model-level zero-to-halt implication under those assumptions, not a theorem
for the distinct retained BFS K0, a reverse implication, or an iPRA endpoint.
A [two-way model join](research/ten-positive-80-symbolic-model-iff-prototype/README.md)
now composes both directions: in each stated IΣ₁ model the positive 80 graph
zero is equivalent to machine halt, with explicit natural β/low/high port
equalities, the complete integer tuple equality, and a separate p₁ model-cast
equality. These hypotheses remain in the theorem. It does not establish an
internal iPRA proof or a binding to the distinct retained BFS coefficient.
A subsequent [port-projection client](research/ten-positive-80-symbolic-model-iff-prototype/PROJECTION.md)
discharges the tuple and model-cast hypotheses using exactly the four natural
symbolic port equalities. This author-checked result remains model-level and
does not bind the external BFS coefficient or prove an internal iPRA theorem.
An [independent iff/port audit](reviews/symbolic-model-iff-row-transfer-independent/README.md)
checks these premises, direct providers and the separate finite row transfer
without replaying Lean.
A [parameterized 80-row iPRA client](research/ten-positive-80-symbolic-ipra-client-prototype/README.md)
now applies the existing completeness adapters to that model bridge. Under
the four natural original-kernel symbolic port equalities it constructs Lean
terms asserting derivability in standard and equality-only iPRA, for the
once-shifted graph and both mapped-refutation-negation and translated source
consistency formulations. This is a guarded author check; a concrete tuple
specialization, full provider-closure review and external file binding remain
separate, and it does not cover the distinct retained BFS kernel.
A [fixed symbolic-port client](research/ten-positive-80-symbolic-ipra-client-prototype/FIXED.md)
now specializes the four hypotheses and provides no-premise Lean statements
of the four standard/reference derivability equivalences for the once-shifted
80-row graph with the original symbolic kernel. The accepted author check is
not yet a complete selected-transitive closure/source-object audit or an
external JSON/PDF binding; it is not a theorem for the different retained
BFS coefficient. An [independent endpoint audit](reviews/direct80-symbolic-ipra-client-independent/README.md)
checks the four theorem statements, positive shift, named theories and selected
direct providers without replaying Lean.
An author-side [serialized-closure scan](reviews/ten-positive-80-fixed-ipra-serialized-closure/README.md)
records 1,765 selected project ancestors with no differing selected-project
overlap. Its producer audit found explicit full direct pins for only 25 of the
157 fresh-header objects; the other producer-time edges, full source ancestry,
package-transitive imports and dynamic dependencies remain unverified. A
[historical producer comparison](reviews/ten-positive-80-nine-prefix-producer-audit/README.md)
found nine direct-import hash drifts across five older producer receipts,
including `ModelArithmetic` versus `ModelDifferenceRing`. A matching
present-day search-path overlap does not establish producer-time compatibility;
the initially observed reverse-dependency cone had 114 modules. Two additional
drifts in [byte-matching adaptive producer records](reviews/ten-positive-80-adaptive-producer-drift/README.md)
expand the combined provisional cone to 122 modules; those historical records
