and display have separate finite checks.

The [29-variable degree-eight candidate](research/adaptive-square-sums29-degree8/README.md)
has finite substitution and degree checks and a matched model replay for
arbitrary integer ports. The initial focused native replay failed because of
broken historical-provider symlinks. A later fresh project-source pair
completed the native endpoint, matched all 1,210 rows and passed an
[independent artifact audit](reviews/fresh-project-pair-independent-audit/README.md),
with the historical and provider qualifications recorded below. A [standard-theory AQ29 extension](research/ipra-standard-aq29/README.md)
also completed one author-only guarded run and an independent artifact audit;
its separate paired Lean replay remains open. The
[28-variable](research/adaptive-square-sums28-degree8/README.md),
[27-variable](research/adaptive-square-sums27-degree8/README.md) and
[26-variable](research/adaptive-square-sums26-degree8/README.md) degree-eight
reductions have finite exact residual and degree checks. Their Lean model
transports and native endpoints completed one [author-only extension](reviews/aq26-author-trial-v2/README.md)
and passed an [independent artifact audit](reviews/aq26-author-independent-audit/README.md).
On 3 November 2026 they, the AQ22 chain and the standard AQ26/AQ29 clients
were [promoted byte for byte into the Lean tree](reviews/aq22-28-current-source-promotion/README.md)
and built there against current sources (35 modules, author build, clean
named axiom reports); the historical runs below remain the paired evidence. The [standard-theory AQ26 client](research/ipra-standard-aq26/README.md)
also completed one author-only single-overlay run and a separate artifact
audit; the later AQ22 pair includes this entire 26-variable chain. The
[22-variable degree-ten candidate](research/adaptive-square-sums22-degree10/README.md)
has independently checked finite substitution and degree calculations and a
[guarded historical author run](reviews/ipra-aq22-guarded-author/README.md)
of its model transport and native and standard iPRA endpoints, with a separate
artifact audit. A later [historical paired run](reviews/ipra-aq22-historical-pair-terminal/README.md)
matched all 37 additions and passed an [independent complete-result audit](reviews/ipra-aq22-historical-pair-independent-audit/README.md).
The standard endpoint uses the
Gödel–Gentzen translation of the selected embedded Foundation consistency
sentence. The
[40-variable quartic candidate](research/adaptive-square-sums40-quotient-quartic/README.md)
has finite substitution and degree checks. A [bounded author-side run](reviews/adaptive-qm40-native-bounded-replay/README.md)
compiled its six model modules, but timed out on the native client. A later
[incremental author-side run](reviews/adaptive-qm40-native-incremental-author/README.md)
compiled the native endpoint and its checks on that model provider; a separate
[artifact audit](reviews/adaptive-qm40-native-incremental-author-artifact-audit/README.md)
passed. A paired replay and complete authenticated closure remain open. A [separate source review](reviews/adaptive-qm40-model-mathematical-source-review/README.md)
found no defect in the inspected model witness maps or residual coverage.
Its [standard-iPRA client](research/ipra-standard-qm40/README.md)
also compiled in a guarded author-side extension on the native provider,
including the selected translated Foundation consistency sentence. A separate
[artifact audit](reviews/ipra-standard-qm40-author-artifact-audit/README.md)
passed; a paired replay and formal PDF-byte interpretation remain open.

For the missing converse of the ordinary 12-variable candidate, a
[single-author compact cell-digit prototype](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/compact44-cell-digit-prototype/README.md)
now proves the desired selector, dummy and copy values and Σ₁ definability
uniformly in the row, dummy bit and position. A later
[single-author word construction](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/compact44-word-constructed-prototype/README.md)
packs definable row and dummy functions into a bounded model word with the
expected local digits. A [model-halt client](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/compact44-halt-packed-word-prototype/README.md)
now constructs the marked packed word at the chosen power dimensions.
The [base-cell low-mask theorem](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/compact44-base-low-nocarry-prototype/README.md)
proves no-carry for each non-forbidden row. The
[word-level low-field client](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/compact44-word-low-fields-prototype/README.md)
now derives both the special origin and all in-range low-mask conditions for
a marked word and for Boolean pulses supported away from zero. The
[exact-product carry client](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/compact44-word-carry-join-prototype/README.md)
constructs the cyclic carry input from the packed word with an explicitly
bounded temporal term; a [temporal-rotation client](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/compact44-word-temporal-carry-prototype/README.md)
derives that term from the word for the exact `(K+2^e)` product. These are
model-level components. The [product-size bound](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/compact44-cell-product-bound-prototype/README.md)
now discharges the kernel-product field for valid base and pulsed cells; the
[chosen-rank client](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/compact44-word-rank-static-fields-prototype/README.md)
uses it and discharges the scalar bounds at the chosen dimensions. Subsequent
[block positivity](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/compact44-word-block-positivity-prototype/README.md)
and [cell-sum](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/compact44-marked-cell-sum-prototype/README.md)
clients establish the remaining size and positivity properties for the base
and Boolean-pulsed marked word. The [all-index high-mask theorem](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/compact44-marked-all-high-prototype/README.md)
now proves ordinary high-mask safety from the checked physical columns and
finite phase cases; the [origin-transfer client](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/compact44-high-origin-marked-prototype/README.md)
supplies the special decremented origin mask. A [halt-to-packing theorem](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/compact44-halt-rank-no-high-prototype/README.md)
therefore derives a full symbolic compact packing and calibrated rank from a
model-internal halt, without separate physical premises, and its [source review](reviews/compact44-halt-rank-no-high-source-review/README.md)
records the exact scope. A [rank-forcing join](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/compact44-rank-residue-join-prototype/README.md)
derives the compact rank population, mod-four phase and size from any such
symbolic packing and identifies the relevant calibration constant. A
[halt-to-seeds-and-transport client](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/compact44-halt-pell-transport-prototype/README.md)
now constructs both positive Pell seed families and a positive shifted
transport quotient for the same calibrated word. A [first-carrier client](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/compact44-halt-first-seed-prototype/README.md)
also constructs a positive first coordinate and its additive transport
identity for those same outer witnesses. Separately,
the exact free-J literal has a checked [factor equivalence](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/free-j-completeness-factor-bridge-prototype/README.md)
and [conditional factor/graph witness bridges](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/free-j-witness-assembly-prototype/README.md).
A later [all-model converse client](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/free-j-halt-model-zero-prototype/README.md)
constructs a positive zero of the exact free-J literal from a model halt,
conditional on four explicit port equalities. Discharging those equalities,
binding the retained/displayed coefficients, joining the opposite implication
and proving the iPRA endpoint remain separate obligations. The components use
guarded single-author providers with inherited ancestry, as described in their
linked records.

The existing ordinary 12-variable candidate has a [source-pinned internalization
plan](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/PLAN.md), an isolated modulo-four Lean
prototype and an exploratory [free-J 95-gate factor split](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/free-j-prototype/README.md)
for arbitrary integer ports in `IΣ₁` models. Its [independent bounded replay
audit](reviews/ordinary12-freej-prototype-independent-audit/README.md) passed;
positive-rank recovery and object-theory proof remain open. Its
first-factor consequence `q³ ∣ Y` for natural ports, a graph zero and positive
`J` and beta has also passed a [separate bounded replay audit](reviews/ordinary12-freej-first-divisibility-independent-audit/README.md);
it assumes neither rank nor checker calibration. The [first-root transport](reviews/ordinary12-freej-first-transport-independent-audit/README.md)
also passed a bounded replay audit under positivity of all twelve inputs and
beta. A later [signed `q⁴` congruence replay](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/free-j-main-rank-congruence-prototype/README.md)
passed a [separate bounded audit](reviews/ordinary12-freej-main-congruence-independent-audit/README.md).
The existence of both Pell indices and the conditional early scalar window
also passed [separate](reviews/ordinary12-freej-pell-indices-independent-audit/README.md)
[audits](reviews/ordinary12-freej-early-window-independent-audit/README.md);
the latter retains explicit model mask bounds. An [early-index bound](reviews/ordinary12-freej-early-index-independent-audit/README.md)
also passed bounded replay audit under those assumptions; it does not identify
the index with the rank. A [separate index-comparison audit](reviews/ordinary12-freej-index-comparison-independent-audit/README.md)
checks the proved ordering and `2R < c`; the [early-size replay](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/free-j-early-size-prototype/README.md)
passed a [separate audit](reviews/ordinary12-freej-early-size-independent-audit/README.md).
A subsequent [bounded model replay](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/free-j-graph-vbound-prototype/README.md)
proves the exact graph's signed `V>c>1` under the stated positive-coordinate
and port bounds; an [independent bounded replay](reviews/ordinary12-freej-vbound-independent-audit/README.md)
also passed. These historical overlays are not yet the denominator-nonzero
theorem or an internal iPRA proof.
The ordinary tail's polarized residual and denominator-cleared identities have
also passed a [bounded replay audit](reviews/ordinary12-freej-ordinary-identities-independent-audit/README.md),
but those identities do not establish denominator divisibility or integrality.
A [signed transport](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/free-j-signed-transport-prototype/README.md)
now derives the unpolarized norm residual in the model difference ring from
the exact free-J graph zero, with an [independent paired replay and graph audit](reviews/ordinary12-freej-signed-transport-independent-audit/README.md).
Separately, the [proof-only denominator's strict negativity](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/free-j-denominator-prototype/README.md)
passed an [independent bounded replay](reviews/ordinary12-freej-denominator-independent-audit/README.md)
under the stated graph and port hypotheses. The denominator is not a literal
graph node. A later [coherent composition](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/free-j-current-denominator-composition/README.md)
derives its nonzeroness together with the cleared auxiliary equation; the
paired rebuild passed an [independent artifact audit](reviews/ordinary12-current-denominator-independent-audit/README.md),
which retains the execution-time manifest qualification. This does not
establish integrality.
A [conditional descent-step audit](reviews/ordinary12-descent-step-independent-audit/README.md)
checks norm preservation and preservation of supplied divisor and Bézout
witnesses; it does not supply those witnesses or prove descent termination.
A [model-level square-divisor theorem](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/model-square-divisor-prototype/README.md)
derives a natural witness for `b² ∣ r` from a supplied reduced norm and
four-natural Bézout certificate, including the zero case. Its paired suffix
passed an [independent artifact audit](reviews/ordinary12-model-square-divisor-independent-audit/README.md).
It does not construct those hypotheses or an internal iPRA derivation.
A [reduced-model descent step](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/reduced-model-descent-prototype/README.md)
establishes norm preservation, strict bounds and signed Bézout transport;
its paired suffix passed an [independent artifact audit](reviews/ordinary12-reduced-model-descent-independent-audit/README.md).
A later [paired measure replay](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/reduced-model-nonzero-prototype/README.md)
constructs a strictly smaller positive model coordinate and passed an
[independent artifact audit](reviews/ordinary12-reduced-model-nonzero-independent-audit/README.md).
A further [simultaneous-state theorem](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/reduced-model-state-prototype/README.md)
combines the smaller coordinate, transformed numerator, norm and certificate;
its paired replay passed an [independent artifact audit](reviews/ordinary12-reduced-model-state-independent-audit/README.md).
A subsequent [model least-number proof](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/reduced-model-induction-prototype/README.md)
proves `b=1` for the stated positive reduced norm and Bézout certificate;
its paired replay passed an [independent artifact audit](reviews/ordinary12-reduced-model-induction-independent-audit/README.md).
A later [natural normalization theorem](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/reduced-model-normalization-prototype/README.md)
constructs the gcd and certificate and derives denominator divisibility from
the stated positive natural cleared norm; its paired replay passed an
[independent artifact audit](reviews/ordinary12-reduced-model-normalization-independent-audit/README.md).
A [signed difference-ring theorem](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/reduced-model-signed-prototype/README.md)
extends this to arbitrary signs and a nonzero denominator; its paired replay
passed an [independent artifact audit](reviews/ordinary12-reduced-model-signed-independent-audit/README.md).
The [free-J graph application](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/free-j-graph-integrality-prototype/README.md)
now proves the proof-only denominator divides the corresponding numerator
under its stated zero, positivity and port hypotheses; its paired replay
passed an [independent artifact audit](reviews/ordinary12-freej-graph-integrality-independent-audit/README.md).
The [graph strong-norm theorem](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/free-j-graph-strong-prototype/README.md)
then constructs a positive natural multiplier without assuming the strong
norm; its paired replay passed an [independent artifact audit](reviews/ordinary12-freej-graph-strong-independent-audit/README.md).
The [graph auxiliary-witness theorem](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/free-j-graph-auxiliary-prototype/README.md)
also constructs positive witnesses and proves the auxiliary norm, retaining
the signed alternatives; its paired replay passed an [independent artifact
audit](reviews/ordinary12-freej-graph-auxiliary-independent-audit/README.md).
The [exact carrier-index theorem](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/free-j-exact-ordinary-prototype/README.md)
then proves `π=R` and `2j=R+1` under those model hypotheses, with an
[independent artifact audit](reviews/ordinary12-freej-exact-ordinary-independent-audit/README.md).
Here `π` indexes the carrier ordinate; the distinct exponent `m` indexing
`f` must not be identified with `π` merely from that statement. The proof of
the later power identity does locally obtain `m=R` for its separately
extracted main index by comparing the same strictly monotone ordinate, though
it does not export that intermediate equality as a standalone theorem. A
later [paired uniqueness client](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/free-j-main-index-unique-prototype/README.md)
now states that any index with that recovered ordinate equals `R` under the
exact-ordinary graph hypotheses, without the later `β≥46` assumption; this
client has passed an [independent artifact audit](reviews/ordinary12-freej-main-index-unique-independent-audit/README.md).
The
[power identity theorem](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/free-j-power-finish-prototype/README.md)
proves `X=2^R` under the graph hypotheses and `β≥46`; its paired replay
passed an [independent artifact audit](reviews/ordinary12-freej-power-finish-independent-audit/README.md).
A [conditional Mersenne cancellation](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/free-j-power-cancel-prototype/README.md)
then proves `q ∣ 2^R` assuming additionally `d≥4` and `β=2^d−1`; its
paired replay passed an [independent artifact audit](reviews/ordinary12-freej-power-cancel-independent-audit/README.md).
Binding these assumptions to the compact44 ports, remaining carrier recovery
and transport of the full argument to iPRA remain open.
A separate [population theorem from ordinary Pell facts](reviews/ordinary12-freej-population-facts-independent-audit/README.md)
has passed paired replay and independent audit. It derives `3h+2 ≤ population R`
from its explicit power, divisibility, index and positivity assumptions, but
does not itself show that the free-J graph provides those facts. A
[source-only closure audit](reviews/ordinary12-freej-population-closure-independent-audit/README.md)
checks the nine-module closure for a graph client. The first protected attempt
stopped on a source style warning before reaching the client. A [reviewed retry](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/free-j-population-client-prototype/README.md)
retained that precise warning, rejected changed or additional warnings, and
completed matching 11-step builds including the graph client on both sides.
The client retains the graph, positivity, port-bound, `β≥46` and `q=2^h`
assumptions; its [independent artifact audit](reviews/ordinary12-freej-population-client-independent-audit/README.md)
passed. Compact44 checker
calibration and the iPRA endpoint remain open.
A [conditional graph saturation client](reviews/ordinary12-freej-saturation-client-independent-audit/README.md)
has also passed a paired build and independent audit. It derives the two
no-carry statements from the graph and explicit radix, repunit, port-split,
mask-bound, residue and mask-population hypotheses. Establishing those facts
for the retained compact44 ports and transporting the decoded computation
remain separate obligations.
A [paired graph-to-symbolic-digit client](reviews/ordinary12-freej-concrete-digits-client-independent-audit/README.md)
combines saturation with the symbolic low-mask decoder and has passed
independent artifact review. It still assumes that the graph low port equals
the symbolic mask, along with its stated radix and mask hypotheses. The audit
records that an older closure-audit rerun fails against the corrected client
source; it separately checked the unchanged closure scan and frozen corrected
source rather than treating that historical rerun as successful.
A [symbolic-mask graph-digit join](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/compact44-symbolic-graph-join-prototype/PAIR-RESULT.md)
combines the graph client with the audited symbolic population, parity and
residue results. Its seven-module-per-side paired replay passed an
[independent artifact audit](reviews/compact44-symbolic-graph-join-independent-audit/README.md).
Both graph-port equalities to symbolic masks and the other stated graph, radix
and product hypotheses remain explicit; this does not calibrate the retained
coefficient tuple or prove a machine simulation.
A [symbolic saturation client](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/compact44-symbolic-saturation-prototype/README.md)
has compiled in one exploratory run and exposes both conditional `NoCarry`
conclusions used by the digit client. Its graph, port, radix, repunit and
product assumptions remain explicit; a combined graph-derived-witness client,
paired replay and independent audit are still open.
A later [fixed-symbolic graph client](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/compact44-fixed-graph-digits-prototype/PAIR_RESULT.md)
has passed a six-module-per-side paired replay and [independent artifact
audit](reviews/compact44-fixed-graph-independent-audit/README.md).
For beta `2^(67*8807)-1` and symbolic masks it derives the port inequalities,
beta lower bound and high-product condition from graph-variable positivity,
while retaining graph zero, radix and repunit hypotheses. `K` remains
arbitrary, and no retained coefficient binding or machine simulation is claimed.
A subsequent [graph-derived radix client](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/compact44-graph-derived-radix-prototype/PAIR_RESULT.md)
has passed a three-module-per-side paired replay and [independent artifact
audit](reviews/compact44-graph-derived-radix-independent-audit/README.md).
For the symbolic beta and masks it derives a positive block count, the radix
and repunit identities and the digit bounds from a graph zero and positive
variables, with arbitrary `K`. It does not bind the retained tuple or establish
the encoded machine or iPRA endpoint.
A [first-root congruence client](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/free-j-first-congruence-prototype/README.md)
has compiled on one exploratory provider. Under its stated graph and port
assumptions it derives the bounded output remainder and the weighted modular
identity used in the proposed [graph-to-decoder route](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/compact44-graph-to-decoder-plan/README.md).
The coefficient interpretation, no-carry transport, decoder and paired
verification remain open.
A [fixed-symbolic composition](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/free-j-symbolic-congruence-prototype/README.md)
has also compiled in one exploratory run. It obtains the radix, repunit,
digit and weighted congruence conclusions using the same witnesses, from a
positive graph zero at symbolic beta and masks, with arbitrary `K`. Retained
coefficient binding, decoder semantics and independent replay remain open.
A [same-witness saturation extension](reviews/compact44-same-witness-saturation-independent-audit/README.md)
has passed a three-module-per-side paired replay and independent artifact audit:
the same `N,w` also supply both global `NoCarry` statements. An earlier direct
composition exceeded the Lean memory limit; the successful proof uses a
smaller generic bridge. A [later isolation review](reviews/compact44-same-witness-freeze-location-note/README.md)
found that a simulated frozen-directory replacement could redirect later
copies before detection. The actual audited source hashes and paired artifacts
are not thereby shown wrong; the original wrapper did not prevent that
intervening write. The retained-tuple binding and model decoder remain open.
A [selector-digit client](reviews/compact44-selector-graph-independent-audit/README.md)
has passed a paired replay and independent artifact audit. For the same symbolic
graph witnesses it derives Boolean selector digits and a zero forbidden digit
at the stated positive, bounded model positions; the origin is handled
separately. Retained-port and full cyclic-word interpretation remain open.
A [selector-period client](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/compact44-selector-period-prototype/README.md)
has compiled in a single-side exploratory run. It derives the selector digit
bound throughout each model period and handles the origin separately; the
forbidden index-1 digit is zero in every period. This remains conditional on
the symbolic graph and positive variables and does not identify the retained
ports or the graph's cyclic columns.
A [supported-slot and copy-position client](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/compact44-copy-support-prototype/README.md)
has compiled in an exploratory combined overlay. It extends the bound to
ordinary supported indices and proves it for the 41 symbolic kernel copy
positions under explicit positive and bounded model-position hypotheses. The
overlay's full companion and transitive ancestry, a coherent paired replay,
retained tuple and graph-column binding remain open.
A [common-period bit client](reviews/compact44-common-period-pair-independent-audit/README.md)
has passed a twelve-module-per-side paired replay and independent artifact
audit. It uses one graph-derived pair `N,w` to bound the selector and all 41
copy digits in each model period, including the origin and forbidden-selector
cases. Retained tuple binding and graph-column identification remain open.
The [two-band rotation client](reviews/compact44-two-band-pair-independent-audit/README.md)
has also passed a paired replay and independent artifact audit. It turns the
weighted congruence into two aligned bands and five bare rotations, assuming
the displayed model decomposition of `K`; it retains `w*C` separately. A
subsequent [graph-to-band client](reviews/compact44-graph-band-pair-independent-audit/README.md)
connects that result to the graph's same witnesses and checks the decomposition
for the distinct symbolic `paramKernel rho`. Its 21-module-per-side replay
and independent artifact audit passed, with the inherited provider and
point-in-time qualifications recorded in that audit. Neither result
identifies the retained coefficient bytes with the symbolic kernel, disposes
of the residual term, or supplies the full graph-column, machine or iPRA
equivalence.
An [external symbolic-K byte check](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/compact44-symbolic-k-external-byte-check/README.md)
reconstructs the parameterized kernel from the pinned finite data and matches
all retained original K bytes; a separate [external review](reviews/compact44-symbolic-k-byte-independent-review/README.md)
matches them by an independent Horner reconstruction. An author-side [finite
kernel certificate](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/compact44-k-finite-certificate-prototype/README.md)
also proves constituent position-separation and coefficient bounds in Lean.
An author-side [generic sparse-radix client](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/compact44-sparse-radix-generic-exploration/README.md)
proves finite weighted-list digit extraction under explicit aggregate bounds,
including duplicate positions. A subsequent author-side [sparse K family](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/compact44-sparse-k-family-exploration/README.md)
proves the symbolic `paramKernel r` is the weighted list sum and its
`Nat.ofDigits` form for arbitrary natural `r`. A later author-side [bound and
digit client](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/compact44-sparse-k-bound-exploration/README.md)
discharges the aggregate coefficient bound for `r ≥ 2^67` using a count bound,
and proves the corresponding digit and size statements without expanding K.
A separate [source review](reviews/compact44-sparse-k-bound-mathematical-source-review/README.md)
finds no defect in the inspected bound and digit argument. A subsequent
author-side [byte-window client](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/compact44-symbolic-k-byte-window-exploration/README.md)
proves the generic little-endian byte formula, including boundaries between
67-bit digits and zero padding, and specializes it to the symbolic kernel.
Its recorded checks include boundary-crossing and out-of-range examples. These
results do not yet establish equality with retained file bytes, gzip decoding,
the other kernel representation or the displayed tuple.
A further author-side [sparse-byte client](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/compact44-symbolic-k-retained-suffix-exploration/README.md)
establishes the symbolic natural kernel's exact bit bounds, a 9,171-byte
middle zero interval and a zero-padded suffix whose last nonzero byte is 8;
the corresponding retained bytes are checked externally, not bound in Lean.
Two further single-side explorations give alternative bounded representations
of that residual. The [bit-rotation client](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/compact44-bit-rotation-prototype/README.md)
represents multiplication by an arbitrary power of two modulo the word modulus
as a bit rotation, with a full radix-minus-one digit cap. The
[carry-input lemmas](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/compact44-band-carry-inputs-prototype/README.md)
instead reduce the residual modulo the word modulus and show that aligned
rotations preserve a supplied digit cap. Both are conditional model results,
not paired replays; neither establishes the clean predecessor, weighted carry
family, retained coefficient binding or internal iPRA endpoint.
The [fixed-graph temporal and generic carry clients](reviews/compact44-graph-temporal-carry-pair-independent-audit/README.md)
have now passed a seven-module-per-side replay and artifact audit. The former
composes the bit rotation with the symbolic graph's same witnesses and actual
exponent without an alignment premise, retaining the full-radix temporal digit
cap. The latter constructs a heterogeneous carry input for finitely many
aligned rotations and one bounded temporal word, assuming the weighted
congruence, output bound and mass inequality. These two clients are separate;
the later fixed-graph carry client below joins the concrete bands. The artifact auditor authored
some clients; the audit is not an independent mathematical review of them.
A separate [model stencil-slack client](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/compact44-model-stencil-slack-prototype/README.md)
checks that carry bound `1` satisfies the required mass inequality whenever
the aligned family's coefficient sum is at most the stated finite stencil
mass; it also checks the corresponding strict half-radix slack. This is an
author-side exploration: the concrete family-sum premise and clean
predecessor are not supplied by the lemma.
The [separated band-mass exploration](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/compact44-separated-band-mass-prototype/README.md)
checks the selector and copy sums and the two-band model inequality needed
for carry bound `1`, together with a generic indexed-family sum identity.
An initial specialization to the concrete flattened family exceeded the
kernel memory limit. A later [indexed-family exploration](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/compact44-indexed-band-mass-prototype/README.md)
splits the specialization into four modules and checks both the exact
concrete family mass and its model carry-one inequality. This is an
author-only result for the formal indexed family; its own statement does not
identify it with the graph's actual rotations and columns.
A subsequent [two-band flattening client](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/compact44-indexed-band-flatten-prototype/README.md)
checks the concrete indexed carry input with carry bound `1` on a coherent
author-side rebuild. It discharges the family mass inequality and flattens
the two rotated bands, while assuming the word and digit bounds, output
bound and split-band congruence. A later
[fixed-graph carry client](reviews/compact44-indexed-graph-carry-pair-independent-audit/README.md)
derives those premises from the same witnesses, yielding the concrete
indexed `CarryInput` with carry bound `1` from positive graph coordinates
and zero at the symbolic ports. Its eleven-module-per-side replay and artifact
audit passed, subject to the stated provider and authorship qualifications.
The temporal-clean and clean-carry clients below give a selected target, a
half-radix predecessor bound and carry reset for the symbolic graph; binding
the retained coefficients remains open.
An author-side [fractional-digit client](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/compact44-fractional-digits-prototype/README.md)
checks, in an arbitrary IΣ₁ model, the formula expressing an unaligned
67-bit quotient window using its two adjacent radix-67 digits. Its application
to cyclic wraparound is supplied by the later window clients; Boolean
adjacent digits alone do not give the strict half-radix bound in every case.
A subsequent [rotation-window exploration](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/compact44-rotation-adjacent-prototype/README.md)
composes this with a non-wrapping bit-rotation window under explicit model
position equations and word bounds. Its theorem does not cover cyclic wrap
or select a clean predecessor from graph data.
A separate author-side [wrapped-window client](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/compact44-wrapped-rotation-prototype/README.md)
checks the wholly wrapped case under explicit index equations and composes it
with the adjacent-digit identity. A third author-side
[straddling-window client](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/compact44-straddling-rotation-prototype/README.md)
checks the boundary-spanning formula and its adjacent-digit composition under
explicit model index and word-bound conditions. Their own statements do not
derive the case conditions from the graph.
An author-side [coverage and all-cases client](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/compact44-rotation-coverage-prototype/README.md)
now combines the three window cases for arbitrary in-range model positions.
It does not itself bound the selected source index by the word length or prove
the graph-specific support and clean-predecessor conditions. A subsequent
[bounded rotation client](reviews/compact44-rotation-windows-pair-independent-audit/README.md)
does bound that index for a reduced cut and records the zero-padded final
digit explicitly. Its fourteen-module-per-side replay and artifact audit
passed, retaining the audit's inherited-provider qualifications. That client
does not itself select a clean predecessor from the graph.
An author-side [clean-phase geometry client](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/compact44-clean-phase-geometry-prototype/README.md)
proves that, for any model shift, one of the two target phases lies outside
the compact blocker `[0,1065] ∪ {1095,2195,3296}`. It uses an interval
arithmetic certificate and bounded-model transfer, rather than enumerating
all shifts. A separate author-side [model support client](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/compact44-clean-model-support-prototype/README.md)
proves symbolic mask support is contained in that blocker and, assuming
positive-index `NoCarry` and an origin bound, obtains zero digits outside
the blocker and Boolean digits away from phase `1063`. A subsequent author-side
[graph/support/carry client](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/compact44-fixed-graph-support-carry-prototype/README.md)
joins those bounds to the same symbolic graph and carry witnesses, proving
the independently obtained lengths equal. Its own statement does not select a
clean predecessor or derive the carry reset.
An author-side [phase-neighbor client](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/compact44-clean-phase-neighbors-prototype/README.md)
also checks that the two preceding phases of any phase outside the blocker
avoid the exceptional position. A separate author-side
[clean-source client](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/compact44-clean-source-select-prototype/README.md)
transports these phase facts to full-word cyclic positions and conditionally
selects a zero source digit with two Boolean predecessors. It assumes the
two source-index phase equations. A later author-side
[fixed-graph safe-source client](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/compact44-fixed-safe-source-prototype/README.md)
obtains the selected zero and Boolean predecessor digits for an arbitrary
model offset from the symbolic graph's same witnesses. The later clean-carry
client connects that offset to the temporal rotation.
An author-side [temporal clean-numerics client](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/compact44-temporal-clean-numerics-prototype/README.md)
checks the model quotient/remainder split for the rotation shift and proves
the zero-target and half-radix predecessor bound assuming explicit two-digit
rotation formulas and the stated zero/Boolean source digits. Those formulas
are still hypotheses of the numerical result, not yet graph-derived facts.
Separate author-side [temporal window formulas](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/compact44-temporal-window-formulas-prototype/README.md)
now cover the aligned, non-wrapping, wholly wrapped and straddling cases under
explicit index and cut equations. Deriving those equations for the selected
graph target remains open. An author-side [cyclic-source branch client](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/compact44-temporal-source-branches-prototype/README.md)
derives the equations for all three fractional source-index orderings. A later
author-side [unified temporal-source client](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/compact44-temporal-source-unified-prototype/README.md)
checks target and cyclic-predecessor formulas for arbitrary in-range model
positions, including aligned shifts. A subsequent author-side
[fixed-graph temporal-clean client](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/compact44-fixed-temporal-clean-prototype/README.md)
combines these formulas with the graph's same witnesses and selects target
`4393` or `5495` where the temporal digit is zero and its cyclic predecessor
is at most `2^66`. The [fixed-graph clean-carry client](reviews/compact44-fixed-clean-carry-pair-independent-audit/README.md)
then proves the incoming carry is zero and the output digit equals the full
raw indexed column at that target. This is a conditional symbolic-graph
model result; its 33-module-per-side replay and independent artifact audit
passed with the recorded provider and authorship qualifications. Identifying the raw column with the
decoder, binding retained coefficients and the internal iPRA endpoint remain open.
A later author-side [clean-column band client](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/compact44-fixed-clean-bands-prototype/README.md)
decomposes that raw column into both selector/copy bands and five bare
rotation terms; the temporal contribution vanishes. Controlling the other
band and bare terms is supplied by a later author-side
[single-band client](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/compact44-fixed-single-band-prototype/README.md):
it proves their source phases lie outside the symbolic support blocker and
obtains the selected decoder-shaped band alone. A subsequent author-side
[selected-input client](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/compact44-selected-inputs-prototype/README.md)
derives Boolean bounds for its selector and copy digits and a `2^41` copy-word
bound using the same graph witness. A further author-side
[local high-mask client](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/compact44-selected-local-nocarry-prototype/README.md)
derives the selected band's required `NoCarry` condition from graph high
saturation. The author-side [selected local decoder](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/compact44-selected-local-decoder-prototype/README.md)
then proves its precise disjunction: either all selector digits and the copy
word are zero, or a selector is chosen and the copy word equals its row's
tile value. A later author-side [origin client](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/compact44-selected-origin-prototype/README.md)
uses the graph's origin digit to rule out the zero alternative and select row
`0` with its corresponding tile value. A further author-side [occupied-bit client](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/compact44-origin-occupied-prototype/README.md)
derives seven Boolean source digits equal to one, including positions `1095`,
`2195` and `3296`. Turning those occupied positions into neighboring decoded
rows, machine semantics, retained coefficient binding and the internal iPRA
endpoint remain open. A generic author-side [decoded-row shift client](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/compact44-decoded-shift-prototype/README.md)
shows that a decoded row satisfying its explicit Boolean and payload hypotheses
has selector `0` occupied at seven cyclic shifts. It does not prove the decoder
premises at those shifted positions. An author-side [overlap check](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/compact44-foreign-origin-overlap-prototype/README.md)
shows a concrete obstruction to reusing the same single-band proof at column
`5488`: its other band contains the nonzero origin digit, and the local high
mask digit there is zero. This is a limitation of that propagation route, not
a contradiction in the graph. An author-side [uniform phase and parity client](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/compact44-uniform-parity-prototype/README.md)
extends support-zero and the target-phase `NoCarry` facts across periods and
shows the output digit is even at positive in-range phases `2195` and `3296`.
Its raw-column evenness corollary still assumes a clean temporal column and
the stated carry conditions at that position; those conditions have not been
derived there. A later author-side [graph carry-parity client](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/compact44-graph-carry-parity-prototype/README.md)
uses the same fixed graph witnesses to prove, at every positive in-range
column of either phase, that the incoming carry is at most `1` and the sum of
the full raw column and incoming carry is even. It assumes no clean column;
the raw term still includes both bands, bare terms and temporal contribution.
A subsequent author-side [uniform parity-copy client](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/compact44-uniform-parity-copy-prototype/README.md)
combines the whole-block shift, reset and Boolean bounds to equate the
appropriate cyclic source digits at both phases. Its indices retain their
model cyclic form; it does not construct a global word or establish machine
semantics. A later author-side [block-copy client](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/compact44-fixed-graph-block-copy-prototype/README.md)
proves for every model `h<N` that the phase-`1095` digit in block `h` equals
the phase-`2195` and phase-`3296` digits in block `cyc(N,h,s)`, including
cyclic wrap. An author-side [occupied-orbit client](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/compact44-occupied-orbit-prototype/README.md)
proves the origin block is selected and selection is closed under the cyclic
`s`-successor. An author-side [internal occupied-orbit client](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/compact44-internal-occupied-orbit-prototype/README.md)
defines the occupancy predicate and proves by model `IΣ₁` induction that
every model step on that orbit is occupied, without coprimality. That client
alone does not prove all blocks occupied or identify `s` as a semantic machine stride. A
subsequent author-side [represented orbit-word client](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/compact44-represented-orbit-word-prototype/README.md)
constructs a model-definable selected-row function and a radix-1024 word of
length `N` for the same graph witnesses. A separate [mathematical review](reviews/compact44-represented-orbit-word-mathematical-review/README.md)
found no gap in the inspected induction, witness matching or word coding; it
is not a paired replay. An author-side [field-bit client](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/compact44-fixed-graph-field-bits-prototype/README.md)
derives same-witness source-bit equalities in the horizontal and vertical
field windows at all applicable model columns. An author-side
[field-marker extension](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/compact44-fixed-graph-field-markers-prototype/README.md)
includes the extra boundary marker positions in those equalities. Translating
the bits to tile-field matching and machine semantics remains open. An
author-side [all-block client](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/compact44-fixed-graph-all-blocks-prototype/README.md)
uses the horizontal marker to prove successor selection and then `IΣ₁`
induction to obtain a selected row at every model block `h<N`. A separate
[mathematical review](reviews/compact44-fixed-graph-all-blocks-mathematical-review/README.md)
found no gap in its inspected successor, wrap or induction argument; it has
not had a paired replay. A subsequent author-side [all-block word client](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/compact44-fixed-graph-all-block-word-prototype/README.md)
packs those rows and excludes the extra row `1` at every block. Field matching,
machine semantics, retained coefficient binding and the iPRA endpoint are not
conclusions of that client. A later author-side [tile-field matching client](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/compact44-fixed-graph-tile-match-prototype/README.md)
proves the horizontal and vertical field equalities for every model block of
the same represented word, including cyclic indexing. A separate [mathematical
review](reviews/compact44-fixed-graph-tile-match-mathematical-review/README.md)
found no gap in the inspected extraction and backward-cyclic matching. It has
not had a paired replay or established machine semantics or the iPRA endpoint.
An author-side [origin-and-match client](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/compact44-fixed-graph-origin-match-prototype/README.md)
also joins the initial row `0` to the same `N`, represented word and field
matches. The existing marked-word provider has a conflicting dependency
object, so a coherent source rebuild is needed before composing it. An
author-side [lookup bridge](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/compact44-field-lookup-bridge-prototype/README.md)
derives the old interface's cyclic matching conditions given the explicit
compact-field cast law, without assuming a positive or coprime stride. It
does not itself rebuild the marked-word provider or supply that cast law. A
subsequent author-side [local compact-word client](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/compact44-local-compact-word-prototype/README.md)
checks a concrete lookup cast and a distinct local word structure on the graph
provider; its identification with the old named interface is still uncompiled.
The [source-closure review](reviews/compact44-graph-marked-join-closure-review/README.md)
and [BusyBeaver addendum](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/compact44-graph-marked-join-plan/BUSYBEAVER-ADDENDUM.md)
record missing historical objects, differing provider objects and additional
source candidates; they are not a completed coherent build. An exploratory
runner has an independently reproduced [provider-shadowing guard gap](reviews/compact44-graph-marked-runner-review/README.md):
the test does not establish live compromise, but the run cannot authenticate
frozen direct resolution or substitute for a guarded paired build.
The [author-side rebuild observation](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/compact44-graph-marked-rebuild-prototype/README.md)
records 81 successful children before `ArithmeticWangTable` exhausted the
3,000 MB kernel limit; the overall build failed and supplies no table object.
A [single-module retry](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/compact44-wangtable-highmem-prototype/README.md)
also failed at the 6,000 MB kernel limit on `rows_goodC`; it does not establish
that declaration or the module. A subsequent [chunked author-side build](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/compact44-wang-table-chunk-explore/AUTHOR_RESULT.md)
compiled all 96 modules and the original-name table module; a separate
29-declaration axiom inventory also completed. Its [independent artifact and
source-assembly audit](reviews/compact44-wang-chunk-author-artifact-independent-audit/README.md)
passed, with exploratory seed/provider and inventory-binary qualifications;
this is not a paired or fully authenticated build.
A [22-module marked suffix](reviews/compact44-marked-suffix-final-author-artifact-independent-audit/README.md)
has now compiled on the author-side table provider and passed an independent
artifact audit. Under positive symbolic graph zero, its graph-halt client
obtains a halt at a model-internal time below `N`; this is not an external
halt or an iPRA proof. Earlier namespace and inventory-import failures and the
isolated corrections remain recorded. The successful build is single-side,
with inherited provider ancestry and receipt qualifications; it is not a
paired or fully authenticated rebuild.
A separate [converse construction plan](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/compact44-converse-construction-plan/README.md)
identifies the compact44-specific packing, calibration and witness assembly
still needed for the halt-to-graph direction; the differently parameterized
2011 construction cannot be applied directly. A six-module [halt-to-compact-word
client](reviews/compact44-halt-compact-author-artifact-independent-audit/README.md)
has since compiled on the author-side provider and passed an independent
artifact audit: a model-internal halt plus its three explicit dimension bounds
gives a definable compact word with finite cyclic neighbor matching. It does
not construct a graph zero. A subsequent [dimensions client](reviews/compact44-halt-dimensions-independent-artifact-review/README.md)
checks those three bounds and two controller size inequalities for the stated
powers, and specializes the word construction under the model halt hypothesis.
That author-side result passed a source/artifact review with the recorded
tooling and provider limitations. Other controller premises and the graph-zero
construction remain separate obligations. A [controller-unit plan](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/compact44-controller-unit-plan/README.md)
records recipe-derived residues and a proposed adaptation of the old
all-model lifting argument. A subsequent author-side [conditional unit client](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/compact44-controller-unit-prototype/README.md)
proves the inverse exists in all IΣ₁ models for the stated power dimensions,
assuming the concrete residue of `K` modulo `342946332593`. It does not prove
that residue for `paramKernel (2^67)`, identify the actual rank variation or
construct the dummy locations; its provider ancestry is inherited.
A separate author-side [Horner-residue client](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/compact44-symbolic-k-residue-prototype/README.md)
proves the required modular residue for the symbolic natural kernel. Its
provider differs from the unit client's, so applying the results together
requires a coherent same-provider rebuild. An [initial join attempt](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/compact44-symbolic-controller-join-prototype/README.md)
successfully rebuilt the four component stages on one author-side provider,
but its final client failed first at the elaboration recursion limit and then
at the kernel memory limit. A later [four-stage separated bridge](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/compact44-controller-abstract-bridge-prototype/README.md)
compiled the actual `paramKernel rho` unit theorem without a residue premise,
using an explicitly identified alias and separate equality transports. This
author-side model result passed an independent [source and artifact audit](reviews/compact44-controller-abstract-bridge-artifact-audit/README.md)
with inherited provider qualifications; the rank-variation identity and
construction obligations remain open.
A subsequent [symbolic rank-calibration client](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/compact44-symbolic-rank-calibration-prototype/README.md)
uses that unit to produce an abstract definable Boolean selector with the
stated congruence, under two explicit size and stride bounds. Its compact
controller source is a mechanically checked triangle-only adaptation; the
single-author run retains its documented linter warnings and inherited
provider limitations. An independent [source and artifact review](reviews/compact44-symbolic-rank-calibration-source-review/README.md)
passed for the inspected transformation and theorem. This does not establish safe physical dummy locations
or the packed-rank variation identity. A [chosen-dimensions client](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/compact44-chosen-controller-calibration-prototype/README.md)
discharges both bounds for explicit powers of `581197`, giving the abstract
calibration for every model computation bound; it inherits the same remaining
physical construction obligations. A [conditional variation client](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/compact44-conditional-rank-variation-prototype/README.md)
checks the exact symbolic rank-variation identity and calibrated congruence
assuming two valid packings, the stated cell perturbation, Boolean and
nonwrapping support, and the calibration congruence. It does not construct
either physical packing or prove the selected changes safe. A separate
[source review](reviews/compact44-rank-variation-mathematical-source-review/README.md)
found no defect in the inspected adaptation or algebra. A further
[realization-conditional client](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/compact44-controller-realizable-bridge-prototype/README.md)
combines chosen calibration with variation, assuming a valid initial packing
and a valid perturbed packing for every admissible selector; it does not prove
that uniform realization assumption. A separate [source review](reviews/compact44-chosen-rank-realization-source-review/README.md)
confirms that the same selector and packing witnesses are used throughout.
The [canonical perturbation client](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/compact44-canonical-perturbation-prototype/README.md)
defines the modified word explicitly, but still assumes that every admissible
perturbation preserves all packing conditions. A [pulse-preservation client](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/compact44-pulse-preservation-prototype/README.md)
reduces those conditions to a selector-independent single-pulse low-mask
fact and five explicit perturbed product and block facts, still assuming a
valid base packing. A checked chosen-rank client applies this reduction with
the five product/block facts required uniformly for admissible selectors. It
does not prove those remaining facts.
A [static-field client](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/compact44-packing-static-fields-prototype/README.md)
proves the chosen-dimension inequalities and three bounds for the exact
symbolic native masks. This exploratory mixed-provider build checks selected
direct overlaps, but inherits transitive and package limitations; the eight
word-dependent `PackingInput` fields and physical dummy safety remain open.
Separate source reviews of the [unit theorem](reviews/compact44-controller-unit-mathematical-source-review/README.md)
and [residue theorem](reviews/compact44-symbolic-k-horner-mathematical-review/README.md)
found no defect in the inspected arguments; these reviews predate the joined
client and do not audit it.
An [occupied-cell prototype](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/compact44-forward-local-cell-prototype/README.md)
proves a conditional local aggregate lemma on an existing author-side
coefficient provider; it does not construct its selector or payload
hypotheses, and its incremental build is not a paired replay or fresh closure.
A subsequent author-side [bounded-row input client](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/compact44-forward-cell-inputs-exploration/README.md)
constructs a one-hot selector and payload bits for each model row below 943
and discharges those local sum hypotheses. Uniform lookup from the compact
word is supplied by a later [same-provider word-cell build](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/compact44-word-cell-author-rebuild/README.md):
it compiles definable selector and payload-bit functions and the occupied
aggregate at every bounded word position. This is an author-side incremental
result; physical packing and the graph-to-iPRA connection remain open.
A later [sparse target-block client](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/compact44-word-target-nocarry-prototype/README.md)
proves no-carry against the symbolic high mask for the two occupied target
phases, including arbitrary model-length repetition. It does not identify
that sparse block with the complete kernel product or settle the other columns.
A conditional [packed-column client](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/compact44-word-product-column-prototype/README.md)
identifies the selected band at both target phases using explicit packed
selector and copy digit hypotheses. Its output equations retain the opposite
band, bare rotations, temporal digit and incoming carry, and assume the
corresponding `CarryInput`. A later [carry-input and packed-digit client](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/compact44-constructed-carry-input-prototype/README.md)
constructs that input for the exact symbolic cyclic product from explicit
radix and temporal bounds and transfers local cell-digit facts to the packed
word. The required cell construction and packed-source bounds remain open.
A [temporal-rotation join](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/compact44-temporal-constructed-carry-prototype/README.md)
derives the temporal bound and cyclic congruence directly from the packed
source bound, retaining the source digit cap and the other column terms.
A separate author-side [band-parity client](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/compact44-graph-band-parity-prototype/README.md)
expands this relation into both selector sums, five bare digits, the temporal
digit and incoming carry; even-weighted copy terms vanish modulo two. It does
not yet prove row propagation. A later author-side [simplified parity client](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/compact44-graph-simple-parity-prototype/README.md)
uses the model support bounds to eliminate both selector sums and all but one
bare digit at those phases. In the first period the occupied origin payload
sets that digit to `1`, so the temporal digit and incoming carry cannot both
be zero at either column. This is conditional on symbolic graph zero, not a
contradiction or a row-propagation theorem. The author-side
[fractional carry-one client](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/compact44-fractional-carry-one-prototype/README.md)
further proves that if the temporal bit-shift remainder is between `1` and
`65`, both temporal digits are even and both incoming carries equal `1`.
That client alone does not exclude those remainders. A later author-side
[fractional exclusion client](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/compact44-fractional-exclusion-prototype/README.md)
combines the carry fact with a model-wide half-radix temporal bound to exclude
remainders `1` through `64` under the symbolic graph hypotheses. Remainders
`0`, `65` and `66` remain open in that result. A separate author-side
[remainder-65 client](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/compact44-fractional-sixty-five-prototype/README.md)
excludes `65` using model cyclic phase separation and the exceptional-digit
bound. A subsequent author-side [uniform alignment client](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/compact44-fixed-graph-aligned-shift-prototype/README.md)
handles every positive remainder and proves that the graph's temporal exponent
is divisible by `67`. It remains conditional on symbolic graph zero and
positive coordinates and has not had a paired replay; a separate
[mathematical review](reviews/compact44-fixed-graph-aligned-shift-mathematical-review/README.md)
found no gap in the inspected argument. An author-side [whole-block shift client](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/compact44-fixed-graph-cell-shift-prototype/README.md)
further derives a reduced displacement of `8807*s` digits with `s<N` for the
same graph witnesses; a separate [mathematical review](reviews/compact44-fixed-graph-cell-shift-mathematical-review/README.md)
found no gap in the inspected argument. An author-side [uniform reset client](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/compact44-fixed-graph-uniform-reset-prototype/README.md)
then proves incoming carry zero at every in-range column of phases `2195`,
`3296`, `4393` and `5495`, and temporal target digit zero at the latter two
decoder phases. It does not assert temporal target zero at the parity phases,
identify `s` with a valid machine stride, or prove a global decoder or iPRA
endpoint. These later clients have not had paired replays.
An author-side [uniform graph decoder](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/compact44-uniform-graph-decoder-prototype/README.md)
now gives the single-band local decoder at every in-range column of phase
`4393` or `5495`, with Boolean inputs and the explicit alternative of an
all-zero configuration or a unique selected row with matching tile value.
It does not establish nonzero rows throughout the word or machine semantics.
A proposed [noncircular integrality-descent route](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/integrality-descent-analysis/README.md)
separates the missing graph inequalities, denominator certificates and
termination argument. Its finite identity checks and independent review do
not establish those unbounded claims or an iPRA derivation.
A [source-current difference-ring rebuild plan](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/difference-ring-build-plan/README.md)
has a separate [source-closure and resource review](reviews/ordinary12-difference-ring-plan-independent-audit/README.md);
two guarded builds of the 70-source coexistence closure subsequently passed
and matched, with an [independent artifact audit](reviews/ordinary12-coexistence-pair-independent-audit/README.md).
The two Lean files added by a later upstream merge are outside that closure;
the 70 pinned sources still matched the workspace at the audit. Provider
transitive/dynamic ancestry and the original Foundation wrapper exit retain
the audit's qualifications. The sampled resource monitor is not a continuous
disk reserve or hard per-build quota. This coexistence check is not an
ordinary-integrality or iPRA proof.
The subsequent graph-specific strong and auxiliary norm constructions and
exact index theorem above address that earlier circularity under their stated
hypotheses. These are model-level results, not an internal iPRA derivation.
The candidate's
compact44-labeled ports include `beta = 2^590069 - 1`, unlike the audited
13-variable 2011 ports with `beta = 2^94517 - 1`. A [finite full-tuple and
parent-graph binding](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/port-binding/README.md)
has been checked; its internal checker calibration remains a separate
prerequisite. The [compact44 calibration survey](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/compact44-calibration/README.md)
identifies parameterized model-level reuse, the hardwired 2011 boundary and
the remaining compiler/controller work. A [single-side exploratory controller-data
specialization](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/compact44-controller-data-prototype/README.md)
now compiles the modular residue and model coordinate surjectivity and
injectivity for width 590069 and modulus 581197, on a historical provider with
one recorded linter warning. It does not establish the full controller choice,
retained coefficient binding or an iPRA endpoint. A separate [controller-choice
specialization](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/compact44-controller-choice-prototype/README.md)
also compiled the choice and conditional rank-calibration statements in an
exploratory mixed-provider build. A later [same-side paired replay](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/compact44-controller-pair-prototype/PAIR_RESULT.md)
passed an [independent artifact audit](reviews/compact44-controller-pair-independent-audit/README.md).
Its endpoint parent was checked using an explicitly contextual historical
source view; the unchanged live-source parent audit still fails on the later
source refactor. No compact graph or retained K binding follows. The local [finite-sum selector/copy
equivalence](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/compact44-decoder-iff-prototype/README.md)
has passed an [independent artifact audit](reviews/compact44-decoder-iff-independent-audit/README.md)
under explicit bounds and an external finite index type. A later
[943-row specialization](reviews/compact44-943-decoder-independent-audit/README.md)
retains those conditional bounds; the [ordered table audit](reviews/compact44-943-table-independent-audit/README.md)
checks the finite Lean list against the pinned JSON externally. Neither result
proves the compiler or controller. A separate [finite relabeling audit](reviews/compact44-color-relabel-independent-audit/README.md)
matches the old 942-row table to 942 compact rows, subject to its explicit
manual source-formula interpretation. A subsequent [paired Lean replay](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/compact44-relabel-pair-prototype/README.md)
checks the corresponding 942-row list equality and all three supplied-array
permutations in 58 modules per side; its [independent artifact audit](reviews/compact44-relabel-pair-independent-audit/README.md)
passed. A [compact-specific bounded model lookup](reviews/compact44-table-lookup-independent-audit/README.md)
also has a paired and independently audited client for the color ranges and
row indices excluding 1. A [paired local row-matching client](reviews/compact44-row-match-independent-audit/README.md)
has also passed independent audit for bounded old-row indices. The external
JSON binding, actual neighbor existence, wrap and full tiling transport remain
separate. A [paired forward marked-word client](reviews/compact44-forward-word-independent-audit/README.md)
transports the supplied cyclic relations of an existing old word. A
[contextually replayed inverse lookup](reviews/compact44-contextual-inverse-independent-audit/README.md)
handles compact indices below 943 other than 1 and has a definability instance.
The [contextual exclusion client](reviews/compact44-contextual-exclusion-artifact-independent-audit/README.md)
rules out row 1 when its stated matching neighbor is supplied, including for
a supplied positive-period cyclic matching word. These replays use the
documented historical source view for their parent audits. A combined paired
reverse-word transport and construction of the required word remain open;
an author-only supplied-word equivalence does not construct one. An
[independent finite recipe reconstruction](reviews/compact44-selector-recipe-independent-audit/README.md)
recomputes the four retained integer payloads and the 8,807 mask digits;
this external calculation is not an all-model port calibration.

The [structured mask theorem](reviews/compact44-structured-mask-independent-audit/README.md)
constructs a block with the required three-way digit shape in an `IΣ₁`
model for an arbitrary support word, using external induction on the standard
block length. Its support is still a parameter: the result does not identify
the block with the retained MC coefficient or give an internal iPRA
construction. A separate [finite-list identity and model transfer](reviews/compact44-list-mask-independent-audit/README.md)
has matching paired replays and an independent audit; it likewise leaves the
specific support and retained-MC graph-port equality open. A subsequent
[concrete support specialization](reviews/compact44-concrete-mask-independent-audit/README.md)
has also passed independent audit: it checks the bit-exponent recipe and its
model digit classification. Identifying this symbolic value with the retained
MC bytes and the graph port remains a separate obligation. A further
[coherent symbolic-mask decoder](reviews/compact44-concrete-increment-independent-audit/README.md)
derives origin digit 1 and digit bounds at model indices below `8807*N` from
`N>0`, `C≥1`, and the explicitly assumed no-carry condition; it does not
derive that condition from the graph. A [paired composition](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/compact44-low-population-exact-prototype/README.md)
also proves the symbolic low-mask population is `589083`, without assuming
the finite support cardinality; its [independent audit](reviews/compact44-low-exact-independent-audit/README.md)
passed, while retained-port identification remains open. A [paired high-mask theorem](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/compact44-high-population-prototype/PAIR_RESULT.md)
proves population `986` for an explicit symbolic digit list and externally
compares its digits with retained `MF_source−β`; its independent artifact
audit passed. A [separate paired residue client](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/compact44-high-residue-prototype/PAIR_RESULT.md)
proves the symbolic value is `4 mod 8`; its [independent artifact audit](reviews/compact44-high-residue-independent-audit/README.md)
passed. The symbolic low-mask [evenness client](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/compact44-low-even-prototype/PAIR_RESULT.md)
has also passed its paired wrapper and [independent artifact audit](reviews/compact44-low-even-independent-audit/README.md).
Internal high-port equality remains open. The [conditional repeated-mask carry join](reviews/compact44-mask-carry-join-independent-audit/README.md)
handles the mixed digit caps under explicit hypotheses, but deriving those
hypotheses from the graph and proving clean temporal alignment remain open.
A [finite fractional-rotation check](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/compact44-fractional-clean-column/README.md)
exhibits a cap-respecting word for which no target has both zero temporal
contribution and predecessor contribution at most one. It also checks a
weaker half-radix predecessor bound for one clean target in every finite
shift/fraction case; its [independent finite audit](reviews/compact44-fractional-clean-independent-audit/README.md)
passed. Neither observation establishes that a graph solution
exists or supplies the arbitrary-model carry reset; applying the weaker bound
requires model-level rotation transport and a sufficiently sharp independent
bound on the other contributions. The author-side fixed-graph clean-carry
client above supplies those ingredients for the symbolic graph.
A [paired heterogeneous-carry prototype](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/compact44-heterogeneous-carry-prototype/README.md)
extends the all-model end-around carry to an individual digit cap for each
word in an external finite family. This allows a rotated temporal word to have
a larger cap than the other words; later clients above specialize the symbolic
graph decomposition and mass bound. Its [independent artifact audit](reviews/compact44-heterogeneous-carry-independent-audit/README.md)
passed with the inherited provider limitations recorded there.
A [conditional clean-predecessor client](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/compact44-clean-carry-reset-prototype/PAIR_RESULT.md)
has passed a paired replay and [independent artifact audit](reviews/compact44-clean-carry-independent-audit/README.md).
It shows that the stated local
predecessor and global carry bounds force the carry into the next column to
zero, and identifies that output digit under a further target-column bound.
The graph-specific decomposition, rotation transport and required inequalities
remain assumptions.
An [independently audited finite coefficient-mass calculation](reviews/compact44-coefficient-mass-independent-audit/README.md)
proves the 943-row selector and 41-copy totals in Lean, with the numerical
half-radix and heterogeneous-carry slack. Later clients above connect those
totals to the symbolic graph's indexed family.
A [finite coefficient-decomposition client](reviews/compact44-coefficient-decomposition-independent-audit/README.md)
has passed a paired replay and independent artifact audit for arbitrary
model-valued selector and copy functions. It does not prove that a graph-derived
cyclic column has that finite decomposition or supply Boolean, copy-bound and

A [local coefficient decoder client](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/compact44-local-coefficient-decoder-prototype/README.md)
has compiled in a same-provider exploratory run: for explicitly Boolean

bound with the conditional decoder equivalence. It does not derive those
inputs or a local no-carry condition from the graph; paired verification remains
open.
A [partial-band rotation client](research/ordinary12-ipra-bridge/compact44-partial-band-rotation-prototype/README.md)
also compiled in a single-side exploration. It relates one aligned reflected

possibly fractional residual rotation, carry reset and retained K binding are

A [conditional two-band congruence](reviews/compact44-two-band-pair-independent-audit/README.md)
has passed a three-module-per-side paired replay and independent artifact

A [direct symbolic-K graph client](reviews/compact44-direct-symbolic-k-pair-independent-audit/README.md)

Its definition is a distinct Lean expression until equality with the retained K

A [manuscript
reconstruction](research/manuscript115-reconstruction/README.md) records that

does not identify the ports.
The [17-variable degree-32 candidate](research/aq22-positive-binding-survey/README.md)
is a further five-binding AQ22 reduction with independently checked finite

The [16-variable degree-32 candidate](research/aq17-positive-signed-binding/README.md)
has a separately audited finite positivity and substitution argument. Its Lean

matched all 52 additions and passed an [independent complete-result audit](reviews/ipra-aq16-historical-retry-pair-independent-audit/README.md).

The [51-variable degree-4 equation](equation4.pdf) has a no-premise

The [44-variable degree-4 equation](equation4-44.pdf) replaces the signed
